Dismiss DragGesture is attached with .gesture on top of the paging TabView

Pitstop/Pitstop/PhotoViewerView.swift:37-52, 69-88 · reviewer: photo-viewer-camera-ui
What it is
The swipe-to-dismiss DragGesture(minimumDistance: 10) is attached with .gesture(dismissDrag) directly to the page-style TabView, so it competes with the TabView's own horizontal paging pan. The handler ignores horizontal-dominant movement in onChanged, but the gesture still recognizes on any 10-point move, horizontal or vertical. onEnded then runs for horizontal swipes too and dismisses if the vertical component passes 100 points, even when the drag was mostly horizontal. isZoomed only updates in the MagnificationGesture's onEnded, so during an active pinch the dismiss drag is not suppressed and the two-finger centroid movement can shift dragOffset.
Why it matters
A diagonal page swipe can dismiss the viewer by accident, and a pinch that drifts vertically moves the whole viewer and fades it out mid-zoom. This makes the viewer feel unreliable.
How to fix
In onEnded, apply the same vertical-dominance check used in onChanged (abs(height) > abs(width)) before calling dismiss(). Use .simultaneousGesture or a higher minimumDistance so paging wins for horizontal motion. Set a 'pinching' flag in the magnification gesture's onChanged (or check magnifyBy != 1) and bail out of the dismiss drag while it is set.
Expected improvement
Horizontal swipes only page, a vertical swipe only dismisses, and pinching never moves or fades the viewer.
Evidence
.offset(y: dragOffset)
.gesture(dismissDrag)
...
.onEnded { value in
    guard !isZoomed else {
        dragOffset = 0; return
    }
    if abs(value.translation.height) > 100 {
        dismiss()
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, medium confidence): I read all of /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/PhotoViewerView.swift (146 lines). The cited code is there and the line numbers are correct. - **TabView setup (lines 37-52):** the TabView uses `.tabViewStyle(.page(...))`, `.offset(y: dragOffset)` and `.gesture(dismissDrag)` (line 50). - **dismissDrag (lines 69-88):** it is `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 10)`. - onChanged checks `!isZoomed` and that vertical movement is larger than horizontal before it sets dragOffset. - onEnded only checks `!isZoomed` and then `abs(value.translation.height) > 100` before calling `dismiss()`. It does not repeat the vertical-dominance check. - So a diagonal swipe (for example 300 points across and 110 down) never moves the view during the drag, but still dismisses it when the finger lifts. That logic flaw is confirmed. - **Pinch claim:** also accurate in the code. isZoomed is only set in MagnificationGesture.onEnded (line 121) and the double-tap / onDisappear handlers. During the first pinch from scale 1.0, isZoomed is false, so the parent drag is not suppressed. Caveats: - Whether the SwiftUI DragGesture actually fires alongside the UIKit-backed page TabView's pan, or blocks or yields to it, depends on the iOS version. That part of the "competes with paging" claim can only be confirmed on a device. The missing check in onEnded is a real code-level defect either way. - How far a two-finger pinch moves dragOffset is also not provable statically. Better fix: the cheapest change is to add the same vertical-dominance check to onEnded, and possibly only dismiss when dragOffset has actually been set. Switching to `.simultaneousGesture` would likely make conflicts with paging worse, not better. A higher minimumDistance, or the dominance check alone, is the safer suggestion. For the pinch, an `@GestureState` / isPinching flag surfaced from ZoomablePhotoPage (or setting isZoomed during `.updating`) would work. This is not a duplicate of any excluded fix.

The cited lines are right: the TabView runs from 37 to 52 with `.gesture(dismissDrag)` at line 50, and `dismissDrag` runs from 69 to 88. Nothing else in the file guards this: no `.simultaneousGesture`, no `highPriorityGesture`, and no check in a caller. Confirmed core defect: the two handlers disagree. `onChanged` (line 73) ignores motion that is mostly horizontal. `onEnded` (line 80) checks only `abs(translation.height) > 100`. So a diagonal swipe that is mostly horizontal, for example 250 points across and 110 down, never moves the viewer (`dragOffset` stays 0) and then dismisses it with no visual warning. That holds whenever the drag gesture gets an end event alongside the TabView's paging pan. Whether SwiftUI's parent `.gesture` and the page TabView's UIKit scroll pan both fire depends on the iOS version, and I can't confirm it by reading the code, so confidence is medium. Overstated part: the pinch claim is speculative. The `MagnificationGesture` is attached with `.gesture` on the child `ZoomablePhotoPage` image. By default SwiftUI gives a child's gesture precedence over the same kind of gesture on its parent, so during an active pinch the parent's dismiss drag is likely blocked. It is true that `isZoomed` only updates in `onEnded` (line 121), but I saw no evidence that the pinch actually moves `dragOffset`. Better fix: the minimal correct fix is to add the same vertical-dominance check to `onEnded`, e.g. `abs(h) > abs(w) && abs(h) > 100`. That alone closes the accidental dismiss. Switching to `.simultaneousGesture` is the wrong suggestion: it would make the two gestures fire together more often, which works against the stated goal of letting paging win. Low severity fits.

Pitstop/Pitstop/PhotoViewerView.swift:107-136 · reviewer: photo-viewer-camera-ui
What it is
The thumbnails in PhotoCarousel get .accessibilityLabel("Photo N of M"), but ZoomablePhotoPage renders Image(uiImage:) with no accessibilityLabel and no zoom accessibility actions. VoiceOver reads each page only as 'image' with no position, and the pinch and double-tap behavior is unreachable for VoiceOver users. Pass index and count into ZoomablePhotoPage and add .accessibilityLabel("Photo \(index + 1) of \(count)") with .accessibilityAddTraits(.isImage) on the page. Optionally add .accessibilityAction(named: "Reset zoom") or a zoom action.

    .scaledToFit()
    .scaleEffect(currentZoom)

Address searches are untracked tasks, so a slow earlier search can move the pin after a newer one

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:343, 757-769 · reviewer: location-lifecycle-and-search
What it is
Each submit starts an unstructured `Task { await searchAddress() }` that is never stored or cancelled. Unlike `geocodeTask`, there is no handle and no in-flight state. If the user submits one query, edits it, and submits again, both searches run. Whichever finishes last sets `mapJump`, so a slow first search can move the pin back to the stale result after the correct one has already landed. A late reply can also set `addressNotFound = true` after a newer query has succeeded.
Why it matters
On a weak connection, where MapKit replies are slow, the pin can end up at the wrong address with nothing on screen to show it. The user may save the Bathroom at that position.
How to fix
Store the search in `@State private var searchTask: Task<Void, Never>?`, cancel the previous task before starting a new one, and check `Task.isCancelled` (or compare the query) before writing `mapJump` or `addressNotFound`. Show the existing "Locating..." style indicator while the search runs.
Expected improvement
Only the latest query can move the pin.
Evidence
.onSubmit { Task { await searchAddress() } }
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift at lines 66-69, 335-358 and 743-770, plus the jumpTo handling in MiniMapPicker.swift (lines 38-45). The finding is accurate on current main. Line 343 is `.onSubmit { Task { await searchAddress() } }`. That task is never stored or cancelled. Only `geocodeTask` (line 69) has a handle, and it is used for reverse geocoding, not for the forward search. `searchAddress()` (lines 757-770) awaits `MKLocalSearch(...).start()` and then writes `mapJump` without checking cancellation or whether the query still matches. If the search fails or returns nothing, it sets `addressNotFound = true`. MiniMapPicker responds to every non-nil `jumpTo` by moving the camera and then setting it back to nil. So whichever search finishes last decides where the map ends up, and a late failure can show "No match" after a newer search succeeded. The `.onChange(of: addressQuery)` reset only runs when the text changes, so it cannot clear a flag that a late reply sets afterwards. Correction on the pin: the jump moves the camera (`position`), and the pin follows only if MiniMapPicker derives `selectedCoordinate` from the camera center. The finding says "pin", which is fine if the picker uses a center-pin design. Scope is narrow. A successful search resigns first responder (line 769), so to trigger the race the user must submit again while the first search is still in flight, or tap back into the field and resubmit. Plausible on a slow connection, so low severity is right. The proposed fix is sound: store a `searchTask`, cancel it before starting a new search, and check `Task.isCancelled` or compare the query before writing any state. Also note that `isGeocoding` drives the "Locating..." text but `searchAddress` never sets it, so the search currently shows no in-flight indicator. This is not a duplicate of any excluded item.

The line numbers are correct. Line 343 is `.onSubmit { Task { await searchAddress() } }`. A stale reply can therefore still write `mapJump` (MiniMapPicker re-centers on it, which moves the pin) or `addressNotFound = true`. After the first successful search the keyboard is dismissed, which shrinks the window further. Low severity is right. Editing a Bathroom overwrites its stored address with a fresh reverse geocode on every save

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:126, 174-175, 800-805 · reviewer: location-lifecycle-and-search
What it is
In edit mode the initializer sets `self.candidate = editing == nil ? candidate : nil`, so `candidateAddress(at:)` always returns nil. `save()` then runs `reverseGeocode(selectedCoordinate)` and only falls back to `editing?.address` when the geocode fails. So any online save of an edited Bathroom replaces the stored address with whatever MapKit returns for the coordinate, even when the pin never moved and the user only changed the rating or notes. A Bathroom that came from a Candidate loses its curated seed address this way the first time it is edited. Before the save, the mini map re-centering on first layout fires the `selectedCoordinate` onChange handlers. That calls `scheduleGeocode`, which swaps the displayed stored address for a geocoded one about 800 ms after the form opens.
Why it matters
SwiftData holds the only copy of user data. An unrelated edit silently replaces an accurate store address (for example, a seed address for a gas station at an intersection) with a reverse geocode, which can be a neighboring building or a different street. Nothing tells the user it happened.
How to fix
Keep the original coordinate in edit mode. In `save()` and `scheduleGeocode`, reuse `editing?.address` when the pin is still within the same 10 m tolerance of the original coordinate that `candidateAddress(at:)` already uses, and reverse geocode only after the pin has actually moved.
Expected improvement
Changing a rating or notes leaves the saved address alone, and seed addresses survive edits. The address changes only when the user moves the pin.
Evidence
self.candidate    = editing == nil ? candidate : nil
...
if let kept = candidateAddress(at: selectedCoordinate) {
            finalAddress = kept
        } else {
            finalAddress = await reverseGeocode(selectedCoordinate) ?? (isEditMode ? editing?.address : nil)

AddBathroomView.save() never calls modelContext.save(), but deletes photo files immediately

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:808-838 · reviewer: add-edit-form
What it is
save() changes the Bathroom (edit mode) or inserts a new one (add mode), then dismisses without ever calling modelContext.save(). It relies on SwiftData's main-context autosave. Before that, in edit mode it deletes removed photos from disk (ImageStorage.deleteImage) and writes new JPEGs. The disk changes are immediate, but the model change that references them only reaches the store at the next autosave.
Why it matters
Suppose the app is killed (crash, force quit, OS kill under memory pressure) after Save but before autosave flushes. Then the store still holds the old imageFileNames, which point at files already deleted, so those thumbnails break for good. Newly written JPEG files with no model reference stay on disk forever. For a new Bathroom the whole record is lost while its photo folder remains. SwiftData is the only copy of user data, so a lost save cannot be recovered.
How to fix
After applying the form state and inserting, call `try modelContext.save()` inside do/catch. Delete the removed image files only after the save succeeds. On failure, keep the sheet open, show an alert, and remove any files written during this attempt.
Expected improvement
A tapped Save is durable before the sheet closes, and disk state never gets ahead of the store.
Evidence
removed.forEach { ImageStorage.deleteImage(bathroomID: bathroom.id, fileName: $0) }
...
            modelContext.insert(bathroom)
        }

        dismiss()
Verifier notes (2)

In add mode, the code inserts with modelContext.insert(bathroom) and then calls dismiss(). There is no modelContext.save() anywhere in the path. A grep over Pitstop/Pitstop finds only one explicit save, `try context.save()` in CandidateImporter.swift:42. PitstopApp.swift builds a ModelContainer and attaches it with .modelContainer(container), so the code depends on mainContext autosave. The ordering problem is real: file deletions happen before the store commits. Scope is overstated, though. SwiftData autosave fires on run-loop and UI events and also when the app goes to the background, so the gap between Save and the flush is short. A typical force-quit through the app switcher backgrounds the app first, which triggers a save. Only a crash or a jetsam kill (the OS killing the app for memory) inside that short gap loses data. That makes the severity low rather than medium. The fix is sound: call `try modelContext.save()` explicitly, and delete removed files only after it succeeds. The same unsaved-delete pattern may exist in other delete flows, but I did not check them.

Dedupe winner is the lexicographically lowest sourceID, so a new nearby listing can swap the surviving Candidate's ID

scripts/seed/build_seed.py:344-364 · reviewer: seed-pipeline-python
What it is
`remove_duplicates` sorts records by sourceID string and keeps the first of each same-group cluster within 50 m. It also drops any fuel-brand station within 50 m of a 7-Eleven. Which record survives therefore depends on what else is in the current run, not on what was shipped before. Examples: a Food Lion (`atp:food_lion_us:...`) appearing within 50 m of an existing Giant (`atp:giant_food_us:...`, same Ahold Delhaize group), or a 7-Eleven newly listed next to an Exxon. In both, the previously shipped record disappears from the seed and a record with a different sourceID takes its place.
Why it matters
Dismissal and visited status are keyed by sourceID. If the user dismissed the old Candidate, it stays hidden, but the new sibling appears at the same spot undismissed, so the dismissal is lost in practice. If the user logged a visit (Bathroom.sourceID links to the old ID), an 'unvisited' Candidate pin now sits on top of their visited Bathroom. This breaks the invariant that a refresh cannot recreate a Candidate the user already handled.
How to fix
Make the winner sticky. Load the previous seed's sourceIDs (already read in `next_version`) and, within a duplicate cluster, prefer a record whose sourceID was in the previous seed before falling back to lowest ID. Apply the same preference to the fuel-at-7-Eleven rule: if the fuel station was in the previous seed and the 7-Eleven is new, keep the fuel station's ID or skip the drop. Add a test that runs twice with an extra nearby feature added on the second run.
Expected improvement
Places the user dismissed or visited keep their sourceID across seed refreshes, so they no longer reappear under a new ID.
Evidence
for record in sorted(records, key=lambda r: r["sourceID"]):
    if any(
        k["_group"] == record["_group"] and meters_between(k, record) <= DUPLICATE_RADIUS_METERS

Background seed import can delete a Candidate the user dismisses during the import

Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateImporter.swift:48-79 · reviewer: concurrency-swiftdata
What it is
`importBundledSeed` runs `CandidateImporter.importIfNewer` in a `Task.detached` with its own `ModelContext` while the UI is already live on the main context. `apply` reads `isDismissed` and the linked `sourceID`s once at the start. If, before the background save, the user dismisses a Candidate that is missing from the new seed (the dismissal is saved by main-context autosave), the importer still sees `isDismissed == false` and deletes it. The two contexts never coordinate.
Why it matters
The design promises that a refresh never deletes a dismissed Candidate. The window is short (the first launch after a seed bump, before import finishes) and needs a Candidate dropped from the seed, so it is unlikely. But if it happens, the user's dismissal is lost silently, and the place comes back if a later seed re-adds it.
How to fix
Have the main UI avoid mutating Candidates until the import finishes (for example an `isImporting` flag set by the app). Another option is to re-fetch `isDismissed` for the deletion candidates just before `context.save()`. Running the import before the scene shows would also work, but would cost launch time.
Expected improvement
The rule that dismissed Candidates are never deleted holds even when the user acts while an import is running.
Evidence
for candidate in existing.values where !candidate.isDismissed && !linkedIDs.contains(candidate.sourceID) {
    context.delete(candidate)
}

The "No public restroom here" button sets `candidate.isDismissed = true` on the main context. Nothing calls save, so the change waits for autosave. Mitigations I looked for and did not find: - No caller guards against the race. - No check is repeated before the background save. - No test covers concurrent contexts. So if the user dismisses a Candidate that the new seed drops, and does it inside the window, the delete goes ahead. The window runs from the importer's fetch to its save. It covers decoding the 1.85 MB CandidateSeed.json and applying several thousand records, so probably about a second, and only on the first launch after a seed version bump. Corrections and qualifiers: 1. The impact is smaller than described. A Candidate missing from the seed is usually a place that closed or was removed. Deleting it hides it from the user, which is the same result they wanted from dismissing it. The dismissal is only truly lost if a later seed adds the same sourceID back. So the visible harm is close to zero, and info would also be a defensible severity. Low is the upper bound. 2. The description says the importer still sees `isDismissed == false` "if the dismissal is saved by main-context autosave before the background save". That is slightly off. The snapshot is taken at fetch time, so any dismissal after the fetch is missed, whether or not autosave has run by the time of the background save. A dismissal that autosaves before the fetch is seen correctly. 3. There is a possible side effect the finding does not mention. After the background delete lands, the main context may still hold the object and later autosave a change to a row that no longer exists. I did not confirm how SwiftData behaves here, whether it raises a conflict error or silently drops the write. 4. The "re-fetch before save" fix only narrows the window. The two contexts still do not coordinate, so it does not close the race. The cheapest complete fix is to have the main UI skip Candidate edits until the import finishes, or to run the import before the scene shows. Given how small the impact is, leaving it as is with a code comment is also reasonable. This finding does not duplicate any of the known or excluded items.
lowConfirmed photos effort: small

Photo files are deleted or created immediately while the SwiftData change waits for autosave, and orphans are never swept

Pitstop/Pitstop/DetailView.swift:261-265 · reviewer: images-and-photos
What it is
Both delete paths (DetailView.deleteBathroom and ListTabView.deleteBathroom at lines 243-247) remove the photo directory right away, then call modelContext.delete(bathroom) without modelContext.save(). The only explicit save in the app is in CandidateImporter. The same applies in reverse in AddBathroomView.save(). Photos are written to Documents/BathroomImages/{id}/ before modelContext.insert(bathroom), followed by dismiss() with no save, and in edit mode removed photos are deleted from disk before the edit is persisted. There is no startup pass that removes BathroomImages subdirectories whose UUID has no matching Bathroom.
Why it matters
If the app is killed or crashes before the autosave fires, the store and the disk disagree. A deleted Bathroom can come back with broken photo tiles, or a newly added Bathroom is lost while its photo directory stays on disk forever with no owner.
How to fix
Call try modelContext.save() right after the delete and insert, and delete the image directory only after the save succeeds. Add a cheap launch-time sweep (off the main thread) that lists BathroomImages subdirectories and removes those whose UUID is not in the set of Bathroom ids, and also removes files not listed in imageFileNames.
Expected improvement
Store and photo directory stay consistent across crashes, and any orphaned photo data is reclaimed automatically.
Evidence
private func deleteBathroom() {
        ImageStorage.deleteAllImages(bathroomID: bathroom.id)
        modelContext.delete(bathroom)
        dismiss()
    }

format_address returns None when a place has no street or city, even though its state is known

What it is
When a feature has no street and no city, `format_address` returns `addr:full`, which OpenStreetMap shops usually lack, and it discards the state and postcode it already has. In the shipped seed, 11 Weis records (for example `osm:node/13165248656`, `osm:node/2112320616`) have `address: null`, even though the Overpass query area gives them state `MD`.
Why it matters
These Candidates show no address in the place detail sheet or list. A state plus postcode (or just 'MD') would at least say where they are.
How to fix
Fall back to `addr:full`, then to the joined `region` (state and postcode), instead of returning None. Alternatively, leave address nil and let the app reverse-geocode those few records.
Expected improvement
No Candidate ships with a null address when the script knows at least its state.

Logging a visit to a dismissed Candidate leaves isDismissed true, so the store keeps saying "no public restroom"

Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateDetailView.swift:74-89, 108-110 · reviewer: candidate-visit-link-seam
What it is
A dismissed Candidate's sheet still offers "I went here". The user can open it with Show dismissed on, and the header there reads "No public restroom". Saving creates a linked Bathroom, but neither AddBathroomView.save() nor the onDismiss handler clears candidate.isDismissed. Afterwards the store holds two contradictory facts about one place: a visited Bathroom (so it has a restroom) and a Candidate flagged as having none. While the link exists the Candidate is hidden, so nothing shows it. The stale flag only matters once the link goes away, and that revival path is reported separately. This finding is only about the inconsistent state left behind at visit time.
Why it matters
isDismissed is user knowledge that is meant to survive refreshes. Leaving a flag the user just disproved means any later logic that reads the Candidate on its own sees wrong information.
How to fix
In candidate mode, set `candidate?.isDismissed = false` next to `bathroom.sourceID = candidate?.sourceID` in save(). A visit proves there is a restroom, so this is a user action rather than an importer write and does not break the never-changed-by-importer rule.
Expected improvement
After a visit the Candidate's flags agree with the Bathroom, so no contradictory state is left in the only copy of user data.
Evidence

    Button { candidate.isDismissed = false } label: {
        Label("Undo: has a public restroom", ...
...
Button { showAddView = true } label: {
    Label("I went here", systemImage: "checkmark.circle.fill")

Error handling & robustness (13)

mediumConfirmedverifier suggests: low photos effort: smallfound independently by 2 reviewers

Failed photo saves are silently dropped and can leave half-written files

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:812-814, 830-832 · reviewer: images-and-photos
What it is
save() calls ImageStorage.saveImage for each selected photo and only appends the file name when it returns non-nil. A failure, whether from directory creation, a nil jpegData, or a write error (for example a full disk), is swallowed with no log or alert, and the form dismisses anyway. Inside ImageStorage.write the full image is written before the thumbnail. If the thumbnail write throws, the function returns nil but the full JPEG is already on disk and never referenced, so it is orphaned. Picker load failures (try? loadTransferable, UIImage(data:) returning nil) are also skipped silently.
Why it matters
The user believes their photos were saved, and they only discover later that some are missing. Photos are irreplaceable user data in an app whose store is the only copy. Orphaned full-size files take up disk space that no code path ever reclaims.
How to fix
Make saveImage throw (or return a Result) and have save() count failures and show an alert before dismissing (or keep the form open). In write(), remove fullURL if the thumbnail write fails. Write with .atomic so a crash mid-write never leaves a truncated JPEG. Surface picker load failures too, at least as a count.
Expected improvement
Photo loss becomes visible to the user, and failed writes no longer leave unreferenced files on disk.
Evidence
for photo in selectedPhotos {
                if let f = ImageStorage.saveImage(photo, bathroomID: bathroom.id) { newFiles.append(f) }
            }
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift, lines 795-838 (save()) and 725-735 (picker load), and all of /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/ImageStorage.swift. Every claim checks out against the current code: - The cited lines are correct. Lines 812-814 (edit path) and 830-832 (new path) both use `if let f = ImageStorage.saveImage(...) { append }`. A nil result is skipped with no log and no alert, and the function always reaches `dismiss()` at line 837. - saveImage (ImageStorage.swift:46-52) returns nil when `try? bathroomDirectory` fails. write() (57-83) returns nil when either jpegData call is nil, and on any write error the `catch { return nil }` swallows it without logging. - The orphan claim holds. Line 70 writes fullData, then line 71 writes thumbData. If line 71 throws, the full JPEG stays on disk and is never added to imageFileNames. Nothing cleans up unreferenced files: deleteImage only removes named files, and deleteAllImages only runs when the bathroom itself is deleted. - Both writes use plain `Data.write(to:)` with no `.atomic` option, so the suggestion to add `.atomic` is valid. - The picker failures at AddBathroomView.swift:731-733 (`try? loadTransferable` and `UIImage(data:)`) are skipped silently, as described. Two scope notes: - In the edit path, the deleted photos are removed from disk (line 809) before the new saves are attempted. That is not data loss, because the user chose to remove those photos. - In practice the failures are rare: a full disk, or jpegData returning nil, which seldom happens for a normal UIImage. Medium severity is reasonable given photos are user data with no other copy, though low would also be defensible. This does not duplicate any excluded item or recent fix.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): What I read: AddBathroomView.swift save() (lines 795-838), the picker loader (lines 728-737), and all of Pitstop/Pitstop/ImageStorage.swift. No per-brand or regional count floor before writing, so a silently empty source wipes a brand from the app

scripts/seed/build_seed.py:160-165, 200-221, 306-341, 397-406 · reviewer: seed-pipeline-python
What it is
The only completeness check is `min_features`, which checks the nationwide feature count of a run before any filtering. Nothing checks how many regional records each brand produced. Several failure modes therefore give zero or a handful of records for a brand with no error: an Overpass answer with an empty `elements` list (for example, the `area["ISO3166-2"="US-VA"]` lookup matches nothing, and that sets no `remark`); a spider changing its `brand:wikidata` or `shop` tag, so every feature lands in the 'another brand' or 'not shop=' skip counters; or a spider dropping `addr:state`. `print_counts` prints the numbers, but the seed is written and its version bumped anyway.
Why it matters
The importer deletes Candidates missing from a newer seed unless they are dismissed or linked. A regenerated seed missing Harris Teeter, Weis or Exxon removes every one of those pins from the app on next launch. The only safeguard is the developer reading the printed counts.
How to fix
Before writing, compare per-brand counts against the previous seed file, which is already read in `next_version`. Exit non-zero if any brand drops by more than a threshold (for example 30%) or goes to zero, unless an `--allow-drop` flag is passed. In `overpass()`, also treat an empty `elements` list as an error.
Expected improvement
A broken upstream source stops the build loudly instead of shipping a seed that deletes a whole brand's Candidates.
Evidence
if feature_counts(run).get(source.spider, 0) >= source.min_features:
...
elements = answer["elements"]
print(f"overpass {state}: {len(elements)} elements")
return elements
...
stats[f"skipped {source.spider}: another brand"] += 1
Verifier notes (2)

The finding is accurate as described. - The only completeness gate is `newest_complete_run` (lines 160-165). It compares the nationwide spider feature count with `min_features` before any filtering. - In `overpass()` (lines 200-221), only a `remark` key counts as an error. An empty `elements` list is printed and returned as success. An area lookup that matches nothing would give zero Harris Teeter or Weis records without any error. - In `build_records` (lines 306-341), features dropped by the shop, wikidata or state checks only increment `stats` counters. - `main` (lines 389-408) calls `print_counts` and then always writes the file and bumps the version through `next_version`. There is no per-brand floor and no comparison against the previous seed. - The importer does delete unprotected Candidates that are missing from the seed (CandidateImporter.swift line 77, tested by `deletesRecordsMissingFromTheSeed`). So the downstream effect is real. Line citations are correct. Two mitigating points argue for lowering severity to low: 1. The script is run by hand by the single developer. It prints per-brand counts by state, and the regenerated CandidateSeed.json is committed to git, so a vanished brand would show up as a large deletion in the diff before shipping. 2. The loss is recoverable. Dismissed and linked Candidates are protected, and the next correct seed reinserts the deleted pins. Unlinked, undismissed Candidates hold no user data. The suggested fix (compare per-brand counts against the previous seed already read in `next_version`, and treat empty Overpass `elements` as an error) is reasonable. One caution: an empty-elements check per state is right for VA, MD and DC today, but it is not right in general for every state.

env_value silently returns an empty string for missing or quoted .env keys

scripts/testflight.sh:21-26 · reviewer: build-release-config
What it is
`env_value` runs `sed -n "s/^KEY=//p" .env`. When a key is missing, sed prints nothing and exits 0, so `set -u` and `set -e` never fire and the variable is set to an empty string. That empty value then goes straight to `-authenticationKeyPath`, `-authenticationKeyID`, `--apiKey`, or the keychain unlock. The parser also keeps surrounding quotes (`ASC_KEY_ID="ABC"` yields `"ABC"`), does not skip `export KEY=` lines, and concatenates the values when a key appears twice.
Why it matters
A typo or a missing line in .env shows up later as an obscure xcodebuild authentication error or a failed keychain unlock, sometimes only after a multi-minute archive. The failure message never says which .env key caused it.
How to fix
After reading the values, check each one and stop with a clear message if any is empty, for example `: "${key_id:?ASC_KEY_ID missing from .env}"`, and do the same for issuer, key_path and the keychain password. Also check that `$key_path` exists (`[ -f "$key_path" ]`). Optionally strip one layer of surrounding quotes in env_value.
Expected improvement
A misconfigured .env fails right away, before archiving, with a message naming the missing key.
Evidence
env_value() { sed -n "s/^$1=//p" .env | tr -d '\r'; }
key_id=$(env_value ASC_KEY_ID)
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/testflight.sh (46 lines). The citation is accurate. Line 21 is `env_value() { sed -n "s/^$1=//p" .env | tr -d '\r'; }`, lines 22-24 read ASC_KEY_ID, ASC_ISSUER_ID and ASC_KEY_PATH, and line 26 builds the auth array. Line 31 (outside the cited range) reads SIGNING_KEYCHAIN_PASSWORD inline. Line 46 passes key_id and issuer to altool. None of these values is checked anywhere. What is confirmed: - A missing key makes sed print nothing and exit 0, so `set -euo pipefail` does not fire and the variable ends up empty. - Surrounding quotes are kept in the value. - `export KEY=` lines do not match the `^KEY=` pattern, so the key reads as missing. - A key that appears twice yields both values joined by a newline. "Concatenates" is close enough. Corrections and narrowing: 1. If the .env file itself is missing, sed exits nonzero and pipefail plus set -e stop the script at line 22, so that case is already handled. 2. The keychain case is overstated. With an empty password, `security unlock-keychain` (line 31) fails right away and set -e stops the script before the archive starts. The "multi-minute archive" delay only applies to empty or wrong ASC_* values reaching xcodebuild or altool. The keychain error also does not name the .env key, so that part of the claim holds. 3. On severity: this is a single-developer release script where the failure is loud, just late and unclear. It does no damage, so low fits better than medium. The suggested fix is sound: `: "${key_id:?ASC_KEY_ID missing from .env}"` for each value, plus `[ -f "$key_path" ]`. For the password, read it into a variable and check it before line 31, since it is currently read inside the argument.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): I read all of /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/testflight.sh (46 lines). env_value is on line 21 and the values are read on lines 22-24. Saving a bathroom waits on a reverse geocode with no deadline

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:796-805, 772-779 · reviewer: concurrency-swiftdata
What it is
When the pin is not on a Candidate's address, `save()` awaits `reverseGeocode(selectedCoordinate)` before it inserts or updates the bathroom and dismisses the view. `reverseGeocode` awaits `MKReverseGeocodingRequest.mapItems` with no timeout or cancellation path. The emergency path enforces a 3-second deadline on MapKit calls, but this path has none. Until the request settles, Save shows the saving state and the sheet stays open.
Why it matters
The app is supposed to work fully offline. With no network the request usually fails fast, but on a weak or captive connection it can hang for a long time. Logging a bathroom then depends on a MapKit network round trip, and the long window also makes the Cancel race in the previous finding much easier to hit.
How to fix
Race the geocode against a short deadline (for example one or two seconds) and fall back to `geocodedAddress` (which the debounced lookup has usually already filled) or the existing address. Another option is to save right away and patch the address in a follow-up task.
Expected improvement
Save finishes within a bounded time no matter the connectivity, in line with the offline-first design.
Evidence
finalAddress = await reverseGeocode(selectedCoordinate) ?? (isEditMode ? editing?.address : nil)
...
guard let request = MKReverseGeocodingRequest(location: location),
      let mapItem = try? await request.mapItems.first else { return nil }
Verifier notes (2)

Nothing races it against a timer and nothing cancels it. - `save()` (lines 796-838) sets `isSaving = true` and cancels the debounced `geocodeTask`. When `candidateAddress(at:)` returns nil, it then awaits `reverseGeocode(selectedCoordinate)` on line 804. The insert or update and the `dismiss()` call (line 838) happen only after that await returns. - `canSave` (line 88) is false while `isSaving` is true, so the Save button stays disabled for the whole wait. - The only deadline in the app is in /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/DrivingTimeLookup.swift (`deadline: Duration = .seconds(3)`, used by the emergency path). Nothing similar exists for geocoding. Corrections and additions: - The cited range should cover the whole function: `save()` runs from line 796 to 838, and the geocode await is on line 804. - The proposed fix is better than the finding suggests, because `save()` never uses `geocodedAddress` today. The fallback is `editing?.address` in edit mode and nil in add mode. `save()` also cancels the debounced task first. So save throws away the address the debounced lookup already found and repeats the same network call. Falling back to `geocodedAddress` (or skipping the second geocode when the pin has not moved since the last successful lookup) would also fix this. - The finding mentions a "Cancel race in the previous finding". That race is a separate finding and I did not check it here. - When there is no network at all, the request usually fails fast and the bathroom saves with a nil address. The long hang only happens on a slow or captive connection, as the finding says. Medium severity fits.

Nothing is lost: the bathroom saves once the call returns, and Cancel still works. Store open calls fatalError with no recovery or backup path, so a failed migration crashes every launch

Pitstop/Pitstop/PitstopApp.swift:11-16 · reviewer: swiftdata-schema-evolution
What it is
PitstopApp.init builds the ModelContainer inside do/catch, and on any error it calls fatalError. The error is never logged through the app's os Logger, the on-disk store files are never copied aside, and no fallback UI exists. The app also has no export feature: grep finds no ShareLink, fileExporter or export code in Pitstop/Pitstop. If a future schema change fails to migrate, or SwiftData rejects the store for some other reason, the app crashes before the first frame on every launch.
Why it matters
The README and the comment on line 8 both say the store is the only copy of the user's data. A launch crash loop leaves the user unable to reach their Bathrooms, photos or notes. The obvious way out is to delete and reinstall the app, which wipes the sandbox, store included. So a recoverable migration error turns into permanent data loss.
How to fix
Replace fatalError with a recovery path. Log the error with Logger, then copy default.store, default.store-wal and default.store-shm from Application Support into a timestamped backup folder under Documents, next to BathroomImages, which survives app updates. Show a minimal error view that explains what happened instead of crashing, and never delete the original files. Optionally add a simple export of the store and the BathroomImages folder so a TestFlight build can pull the data off before any fix ships.
Expected improvement
A failed migration leaves the app running with a backup of the store on disk, so the data can be recovered with a later build instead of being lost to a reinstall.
Evidence
do {
            container = try ModelContainer(for: Bathroom.self, Candidate.self)
        } catch {
            fatalError("Could not open the SwiftData store: \(error)")
        }

A grep of Pitstop/Pitstop for ShareLink, fileExporter, export and Logger returns only that line-34 hit, so the claim that no export path exists holds. The finding does not duplicate any excluded item or recent fix. It also does not conflict with the intentional designs. The design rule is "migration, never a wipe", and crashing on launch with no backup is the failure mode that rule is meant to prevent. Minor corrections. The cited range 11-16 covers init up to the end of the catch; the fatalError itself is on line 15. The trigger is hypothetical today: it needs a future schema change that fails to migrate or a corrupt store, so medium is a fair rating rather than high. There is one point to note about the suggested fix. ModelContainer(for:) with the default configuration puts default.store in Application Support. The only schema change so far is the optional sourceID field, which lightweight migration handles. Crashing on container creation is also Apple's standard SwiftData template pattern. (4) In a single-developer app the developer can pull the app's container through Xcode for builds installed from Xcode, though not for TestFlight or App Store builds. The issue is real but moderate: there is no logged, non-crashing recovery path and no way to get the data out. I suggest lowering severity to low. The proposed fix is reasonable, with one caveat. The cheaper and more important guard is to add a VersionedSchema with a SchemaMigrationPlan before the next schema change, and to test that migration on a copy of a real store.

CameraPickerView sets sourceType = .camera without checking availability or authorization

Pitstop/Pitstop/CameraPickerView.swift:14-20 · reviewer: photo-viewer-camera-ui
What it is
makeUIViewController sets picker.sourceType = .camera unconditionally. There is no UIImagePickerController.isSourceTypeAvailable(.camera) check and no AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus(for: .video) check anywhere in the target (a grep finds neither). The Camera button in AddBathroomView (around line 624) is only disabled when totalPhotoCount >= 10, and the fullScreenCover at lines 296-301 always presents CameraPickerView. UIKit throws NSInvalidArgumentException when .camera is assigned and the source type is unavailable, which is the case in the Simulator and when the camera is restricted by Screen Time or a device management profile. If the user has denied camera permission, the picker opens on the system's black 'no access' screen and the app gives no route to Settings.
Why it matters
Tapping Camera in the Simulator, or on a device where the camera is restricted, crashes the app and loses the half-filled Add Bathroom form. When permission is denied, the user sees a dead full-screen black view with no explanation and no way to fix it from the app.
How to fix
Hide or disable the Camera button when UIImagePickerController.isSourceTypeAvailable(.camera) is false. Before setting showCamera = true, check AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus(for: .video): for .notDetermined call requestAccess, for .denied or .restricted show an alert that links to UIApplication.openSettingsURLString, and present the picker only when the status is .authorized. As a backstop, guard inside makeUIViewController so it falls back to .photoLibrary and never crashes.
Expected improvement
No crash in the Simulator or on restricted devices, and users who denied camera access get a clear message and a one-tap path to Settings instead of a black screen.
Evidence
let picker = UIImagePickerController()
        picker.sourceType = .camera
...

}
.disabled(totalPhotoCount >= 10)

Seed import failures log a redacted, generic message and are otherwise silent

Pitstop/Pitstop/PitstopApp.swift:33-36 · reviewer: seed-import-correctness
What it is
Any failure to load, decode or save the seed goes to one os.Logger call that interpolates error.localizedDescription with no privacy annotation. Dynamic strings in os_log are private by default, so on a TestFlight device without a debugger attached the message reads '<private>'. For a DecodingError, localizedDescription is also only the generic 'The data couldn't be read because it isn't in the correct format', which drops the codingPath and the failing record. The whole seed decodes as one unit, so one bad record means zero Candidates. The app then retries and fails on every launch, and the user sees nothing.
Why it matters
A broken seed shipped through TestFlight shows up only as an empty Candidates layer, and the device logs cannot say why. That makes the failure hard to diagnose after release.
How to fix
Log String(describing: error) with privacy: .public, which includes the DecodingError context and codingPath. The bundled-seed unit test already guards decoding at build time, so keeping the log is enough. Add a small user-visible sign if wanted.
Expected improvement
A seed import failure on a device can be diagnosed from Console logs, down to the failing key path.
Evidence
Logger(subsystem: "com.danielgentile.GeoPoop", category: "CandidateImporter")
                    .error("Seed import failed: \(error.localizedDescription)")

Correction on scope: "one bad record means zero Candidates" holds only on a fresh install or when no seed was ever imported. Directions open failures are silent; the maps:// fallback is effectively dead code

Pitstop/Pitstop/MapURLHelper.swift:32-42 · reviewer: emergency-ranking
What it is
`canOpenURL` always returns true for an https URL, so the `maps://` fallback is never tried. Nothing passes a completion to `UIApplication.shared.open`, so a failed open (or a URL that fails to parse) ends the function with no feedback. In the emergency flow the spinner stops and nothing happens.
Why it matters
In an emergency, a tap that silently does nothing is the worst outcome. The fallback list suggests a robustness the code does not deliver.
How to fix
Use `open(_:options:completionHandler:)` and try the next candidate URL, or report back to the caller so ContentView can alert, when `success` is false. Alternatively, drop the fallback and use `MKMapItem.openInMaps(launchOptions: [MKLaunchOptionsDirectionsModeKey: MKLaunchOptionsDirectionsModeDriving])`, which needs no URL building or scheme checks.
Expected improvement
A failed handoff to Maps is either retried or shown to the user rather than ignored.
Evidence
`if let url = URL(string: urlString), UIApplication.shared.canOpenURL(url) {
                UIApplication.shared.open(url)
                return
            }`
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/MapURLHelper.swift in full (44 lines). Lines 32-42 match the finding exactly. The loop opens the first one where `URL(string:)` succeeds and `canOpenURL` returns true, then returns. `UIApplication.shared.open(url)` gets no completion handler, and the function returns Void, so callers learn nothing. Callers: ContentView.swift:190 (emergency, `.driving`), CandidateDetailView.swift:146 (`.driving`) and DetailView.swift:258 (default `.walking`). In ContentView the only alert is the "no ranked result" case at lines 185-188. Nothing reports a failed open. The claim holds: `canOpenURL` with an http/https URL returns true on iOS and needs no LSApplicationQueriesSchemes entry, so maps:// is effectively never reached. Corrections: 1. The URL-parse failure path is close to impossible. Both strings are built from fixed-point numbers and fixed tokens, so `URL(string:)` will not return nil in practice. 2. A failed open of an https maps.apple.com link is also very unlikely, because iOS falls back to Safari if Maps is missing. The real effect is small: dead fallback code plus no error path, not a realistic emergency failure. 3. I did not check the "spinner stops" detail in ContentView beyond lines 185-190. Severity: low, or arguably info. Of the proposed fixes, the cleanest is `MKMapItem.openInMaps` with a directions-mode launch option, which needs no URL building or scheme checks.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, medium confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/MapURLHelper.swift in full (the loop is at lines 37-42, the candidate URLs at 32-35), its three callers (ContentView.swift:190, CandidateDetailView.swift:146, DetailView.swift:258), handleEmergencyTap in ContentView.swift lines 143-191, and the INFOPLIST_KEY_* build settings in project.pbxproj. What holds up: the maps:// fallback is dead code. canOpenURL returns true for an https URL on iOS, so the first candidate always wins. The fallback is dead for a second reason the finding misses: no LSApplicationQueriesSchemes entry exists in the build settings, so canOpenURL would return false for maps:// even if the loop reached it. In ContentView the spinner (isFindingRoute) is already cleared before ranking, and the "no results" case has its own alert, so the real silent-failure surface is only UIApplication.open returning false. Better fix: delete the candidate list and loop. Either open the https URL directly or, cleaner, use MKMapItem.openInMaps with MKLaunchOptionsDirectionsModeKey, which takes the transport mode and needs no URL building or scheme checks. Adding a completion handler alert is optional. This is a cleanup item, so I would downgrade it from low to info.

Open detail sheet and pin annotations keep live SwiftData models that the background seed import can delete

Pitstop/Pitstop/ClusteredMapView.swift:259-270 (and MapTabView.swift 79-81, PlaceDetailSheet.swift 13-18) · reviewer: map-clustering
What it is
`PlaceAnnotation.place` stores the `MapSelection`, which wraps the live `Candidate` or `Bathroom` model. `didSelect` passes that object to the sheet, and `PlaceDetailSheet` renders `CandidateDetailView(candidate:)` from it. The seed import runs in `Task.detached` on its own ModelContext (PitstopApp.swift 28-29) and deletes Candidates missing from a newer seed. If the user opens such a Candidate's sheet during the first launch after an update, or taps its pin before the main context merges the change and @Query refreshes, the sheet keeps reading properties of a model whose backing row is gone. SwiftData can trap on that. The appearance code itself is safe because it uses the `PinLook` snapshot; only the selection path holds the live model.
Why it matters
The window is narrow (one launch per seed bump, and only Candidates dropped from the seed), but if it hits, the app crashes. That is the outcome the snapshot design was meant to avoid.
How to fix
Store the `PersistentIdentifier` in `MapSelection` and resolve it on the main context when the sheet builds (`modelContext.model(for:)` or a fetch by sourceID). Show nothing and clear the selection when the model is missing or `isDeleted`. Alternatively, guard in PlaceDetailSheet with `candidate.isDeleted` / `modelContext == nil` and dismiss.
Expected improvement
A Candidate deleted by the seed import while its pin or sheet is on screen closes cleanly instead of risking a crash.
Evidence
final class PlaceAnnotation: NSObject, MKAnnotation {

    let place: MapSelection
...
            } else if let place = annotation as? PlaceAnnotation {
                parent.onSelect(place.place)

Dismiss and undo of "No public restroom here" rely on autosave with no explicit save

Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateDetailView.swift:82-100 · reviewer: detail-views-ui-state
What it is
Both buttons flip `candidate.isDismissed` on the main context and, for dismiss, close the sheet right away. Nothing calls `modelContext.save()`, so the change persists only when SwiftData's autosave next runs. The UI updates immediately because Candidate is an `@Model`, but if the app is killed (crash, force quit, or a TestFlight build being replaced) before autosave, the dismissal is lost. The seed importer's rule "deletes missing Candidates unless dismissed" reads the persisted flag from its own background context, so an unsaved dismissal is also invisible to it.
Why it matters
Dismissal is the one user decision that protects a Candidate from deletion on the next seed refresh, and the user has no way to tell whether it was saved.
How to fix
Call `try? modelContext.save()` (or log the error) right after setting `isDismissed` in both buttons. The same pattern could go after insert and edit in AddBathroomView, which also has no explicit save.
Expected improvement
Dismiss and undo are durable the moment the user taps them.
Evidence
Button { candidate.isDismissed = false } label: {
...
Button {
    candidate.isDismissed = true
    dismiss()
}

The claim that the importer "can't see" an unsaved dismissal is technically true but adds nothing beyond that kill-before-autosave case. lowConfirmed seed-pipeline effort: small

All The Places downloads have no retry, and history.json is downloaded again for every source

scripts/seed/build_seed.py:58-64, 154-157, 207-221 · reviewer: seed-pipeline-python
What it is
`AtpSource.load` calls `fetch_json(ATP_RUN_HISTORY)` once per source with no caching, so the same history file is downloaded about 27 times per build, and runs published mid-build can be seen by some sources and not others. `fetch_json` has no retry. One transient HTTP error on any of the large spider GeoJSON downloads (shell is worldwide, about 49k features) aborts the whole build with a traceback, after minutes of downloads, while the Overpass path has a 5-try backoff. That backoff in `overpass()` has its own flaws. It also sleeps 240 s after the final failed attempt before calling `sys.exit`. It retries non-transient failures such as an HTTP 400 for a malformed query.
Why it matters
Network builds are slow and flaky for avoidable reasons. Nothing is written on failure, so the seed is never corrupted, but every failure means starting the whole build over.
How to fix
Wrap the history fetch in `@cache` (or fetch it once in `main` and pass it in). Move the retry-with-backoff loop into `fetch_json` so all network calls share it. Skip the sleep after the last attempt, and do not retry 4xx responses other than 429.
Expected improvement
Fewer redundant requests, every source sees the same run history, and transient errors no longer abort the build.
Evidence
run = newest_complete_run(self, fetch_json(ATP_RUN_HISTORY), lambda r: run_feature_counts(r["stats_url"]))
...
wait = 15 * 2**attempt
print(f"overpass {state}: {error}, retrying in {wait} s")
time.sleep(wait)
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/seed/build_seed.py at lines 25-70, 150-225 and 389-412. Every claim in the finding holds. - **History file fetched once per source:** `AtpSource.load` (lines 58-64, call on line 61) runs `fetch_json(ATP_RUN_HISTORY)` with no cache. `main` calls `source.load` for every entry in SOURCES, and there are 27 `AtpSource(...)` entries, so the file is downloaded 27 times per network build. `run_feature_counts` (line 168) and `osm_features` (line 173) are wrapped in `@cache`, but the history fetch is not. Since each source reads its own copy, a run published mid-build can be seen by later sources and not earlier ones, as the finding says. - **No retry in `fetch_json`:** lines 154-157 are a bare `urlopen` with a 120 s timeout. Any HTTP or connection error on a spider GeoJSON download ends the build with a traceback. Nothing is written in that case, because the output write happens at the end of `main`. - **Overpass backoff flaws:** `overpass()` (lines 200-221) tries 5 times and waits `15 * 2**attempt`. On the last attempt (attempt=4) it sleeps 240 s and only then reaches `sys.exit`. It catches `(OSError, ValueError)`. `HTTPError` is a subclass of `URLError`, which is a subclass of `OSError`, so a 400 for a bad query is retried along with 429 and 504. - **Shell size:** the "about 49k features" figure matches `min_features=49_000` on line 119. Whether that scrape covers the whole world is not visible in the code, but it doesn't affect the finding. Line citations are accurate. The suggested fix is sound. A slightly better option is to fetch the history once in `main`, or put it behind `@cache`, so every source sees the same snapshot; that also removes the mid-build inconsistency. This does not duplicate any excluded or recently fixed item.

One caution: a shared retry in fetch_json would multiply the wait time on the large geojson downloads, so keep the attempt count small.

Address search shows "No match for that address" when it is offline or MapKit returns an error

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:757-766, 348-349 · reviewer: location-lifecycle-and-search
What it is
`searchAddress()` wraps `MKLocalSearch(request:).start()` in `try?` and treats both an empty `mapItems` and a thrown error (no network, throttling, MKError.serverFailure) as `addressNotFound = true`. The row then reads "No match for that address" in red. Offline, every query reports that the address does not exist, and the field gives no sign that the search needs a connection. The search has a 50 km region bias around `selectedCoordinate`, but the bias is a hint, not a filter, and `.first` is taken without checking distance. So a common street name can jump the pin to another state with no confirmation.
Why it matters
The app is meant to work offline, and address search is the one documented network feature in this form. Telling an offline user their address does not exist leads them to retype a correct address over and over instead of dragging the pin.
How to fix
Use `do/catch`. On a thrown error, set a separate state such as `addressSearchFailed`, and show "Search needs a connection. Drag the map instead." Keep "No match" for an empty result. You can also skip `mapItems` results far outside the request region, or warn when the first match is more than about 50 km from the current pin.
Expected improvement
Offline users are told why search failed and to drag the map instead, and an unexpected far-away match no longer moves the pin silently.
Evidence
guard let item = try? await MKLocalSearch(request: request).start().mapItems.first else {
            addressNotFound = true
            return
        }

MKLocalSearch usually reports "no results" by throwing MKError.placemarkNotFound, not by returning an empty mapItems array. mediumConfirmed photos effort: mediumfound independently by 3 reviewers

All photo disk IO and JPEG encode/decode runs on the main actor, and the async loaders never leave it

Pitstop/Pitstop/ImageStorage.swift:46-119 · reviewer: images-and-photos
What it is
The target builds with SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor (project.pbxproj lines 351 and 389), so the ImageStorage enum and all its static methods are main-actor isolated. loadThumbnailAsync and loadImageAsync are marked async but contain no suspension point. They call the synchronous loadThumbnail/loadImage, which read the file with UIImage(contentsOfFile:) on the main thread. Saving is worse: AddBathroomView.save() loops over up to 10 selected photos and calls ImageStorage.saveImage for each, which resizes twice and JPEG-encodes twice on the main thread before dismiss() runs.
Why it matters
Saving a Bathroom with several photos freezes the UI for the time it takes to resize and encode them (made worse by the 3x renderer scale). Opening a Bathroom with photos or scrolling the list does synchronous file reads on the main thread. The async signatures suggest the work is off the main thread when it is not.
How to fix
Mark the pure file and encode helpers nonisolated (or move them into a nonisolated helper or an actor), and in the async variants do the read and decode inside a detached task or a @concurrent function, returning the UIImage to the caller. Use UIImage.byPreparingThumbnail(ofSize:) or prepareForDisplay to decode off the main thread. In save(), run the resize/encode/write loop off the main actor and only assign imageFileNames back on the main actor. The two key-tracking dictionaries would then need actor or lock protection.
Expected improvement
Saving with photos and loading thumbnails no longer block the main thread, so the form dismisses promptly and list scrolling stays smooth.
Evidence
static func loadThumbnailAsync(bathroomID: UUID, fileName: String) async -> UIImage? {
        let key = cacheKey(bathroomID: bathroomID, fileName: fileName, isThumb: true)
        if let cached = thumbnailCache.object(forKey: key) { return cached }
        return loadThumbnail(bathroomID: bathroomID, fileName: fileName)
    }

They call the synchronous `loadThumbnail` and `loadImage`, which call `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` (lines 92 and 103) and also run `bathroomDirectory`, which calls `createDirectory` on every load. There is also a direct synchronous `ImageStorage.loadThumbnail` call at AddBathroomView.swift:668, during view building for existing photos in edit mode. It is in the same class of problem. 3. A related point the finding only touches: `UIGraphicsImageRenderer(size:)` uses the default format, whose scale is the screen scale (3x). So the "1200px" full image is really stored at about 3600px and the "200px" thumbnail at about 600px. That makes the encode cost and file sizes bigger than the constants suggest. It could be a separate finding: set `format.scale = 1`. 4. `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` decodes lazily, so the main-thread cost at load time is the file read plus a later decode at first render. That is why the suggested `byPreparingThumbnail` or `prepareForDisplay` fix is the right one. Medium severity fits: several photos at 3x scale is a visible freeze on save.

List tab re-filters and re-sorts all ~6,000 Candidates on every GPS fix

Pitstop/Pitstop/ListTabView.swift:42-58, 107-117, 203-212 · reviewer: filters-and-list
What it is
`displayedPlaces` reads `locationManager.userLocation` from an `@Observable` LocationManager. LocationManager sets no `distanceFilter`, so every location callback (about once a second while moving, plus jitter when standing still) invalidates the List body. Each pass runs `unlinked(from:)` over all 6,055 seed Candidates, calls `filter.applies` on each, allocates two CLLocations per place in `isWithinMaxDistance` when a radius is set, allocates another CLLocation per place for the distance sort, sorts O(n log n), and makes SwiftUI diff a ForEach of thousands of identities. Each visible row also builds a new `MKDistanceFormatter` on every render. The `.animation(..., value: searchText)` modifier also animates that diff on every search keystroke.
Why it matters
The default state (Show unvisited on, sorted by distance) puts every Candidate in the list, so this work runs at full size whenever the List tab is open and the phone is moving, which costs scrolling smoothness and battery. The code comment "Computed once per render; there can be thousands of Candidates" shows the scale is known, but renders are driven by GPS rather than by data changes.
How to fix
Set `manager.distanceFilter` (for example 25 to 50 meters) in LocationManager so small jitter does not republish, or quantize the location the list depends on. Cache the filtered and sorted result in `@State` and recompute it in `.task(id:)` or `.onChange` keyed on (filter, searchText, sortOption, coarse location, query counts) instead of in `body`. Compute each place's distance once and pass it into the rows instead of having each row recompute it, and use one static `MKDistanceFormatter`.
Expected improvement
The list is recomputed only when inputs change in a meaningful way, rows stop allocating formatters, and scrolling stays smooth while driving.
Evidence
private var displayedPlaces: [MapSelection] {
        let userLocation = locationManager.userLocation
...
        let unvisited = candidates.unlinked(from: bathrooms).filter {

(LocationManager.swift) manager.desiredAccuracy = kCLLocationAccuracyNearestTenMeters   // no distanceFilter set

Every GPS fix and every region change re-runs the full filter and a 6k-entry annotation diff on the main thread

Pitstop/Pitstop/ClusteredMapView.swift:35-39, 88-113 (plus MapTabView.swift 33-37, 53-59; LocationManager.swift 26-28, 56-59) · reviewer: map-clustering
What it is
`filteredCandidates` reads `locationManager.userLocation` for every candidate. It is passed as an argument even when `maxDistance` is nil, so MapTabView's body depends on every location update. LocationManager sets no `distanceFilter` and assigns `userLocation` on every `didUpdateLocations`, which is about once a second while moving. The coordinator's `regionDidChange` also writes the camera binding back after every pan, and on every follow-mode camera move. Each of these re-evaluates MapTabView: `unlinked(from:)` builds a Set and filters about 6,055 seed Candidates. Then `updateUIView` calls `syncAnnotations`, which builds a fresh `[PersistentIdentifier: MapSelection]` dictionary over every place. It also constructs a `PinLook` for each one: several SwiftData property reads, a BrandProfile table lookup and an accessibility-label string interpolation. The `onSelect: select` closure is new on each render, so SwiftUI cannot skip the representable update.
Why it matters
While driving with the map following the user, which is the emergency use case, the main thread rebuilds tens of thousands of SwiftData property reads and thousands of strings once or twice a second just to learn that nothing changed. That costs scroll and pan smoothness and battery on the screen that is meant to stay smooth with thousands of pins.
How to fix
Read `userLocation` only when `filter.maxDistance != nil`, for example `let origin = filter.maxDistance == nil ? nil : locationManager.userLocation`. Set `manager.distanceFilter` (for example 25 to 50 m) in LocationManager. In `syncAnnotations`, short-circuit when the input has not changed: cache a cheap fingerprint of the last input (counts plus the arrays' identifiers, or a version bumped by @Query changes) and return early. Build the accessibility label lazily in `prepareForDisplay` instead of storing it in `PinLook`.
Expected improvement
Location ticks and camera write-backs stop costing O(n) main-thread work when the visible set has not changed. Annotation diffing runs only when the data or the filter actually changes.
Evidence
filter.applies(to: $0) && filter.isWithinMaxDistance($0.coordinate, of: locationManager.userLocation)
...
for candidate in parent.candidates { wanted[candidate.persistentModelID] = .candidate(candidate) }
...
for (id, place) in wanted {
    let look = PinLook(place)

Photo picker keeps up to 10 full-resolution images in @State and renders them into 72pt tiles

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:690-700, 726-737 · reviewer: images-and-photos
What it is
loadPickerItems loads each PhotosPickerItem as raw Data, builds a full-resolution UIImage(data:) (often a 12 MP or 48 MP HEIC), and appends it to selectedPhotos. newPhotoThumbnail then draws that full UIImage with Image(uiImage:).resizable() in a 72x72 frame, which forces a full-size decode of every selected photo. The camera path appends the full originalImage the same way. Nothing is downsampled until ImageStorage.saveImage runs at save time.
Why it matters
A decoded 12 MP image is about 48 MB and a 48 MP one is about 190 MB. Picking 10 library photos can push the form to hundreds of megabytes and risk a memory-pressure termination while the user is still filling in the form, which loses everything they entered.
How to fix
Downsample when loading. Use CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex with kCGImageSourceThumbnailMaxPixelSize of about 1200 and kCGImageSourceCreateThumbnailWithTransform = true on the picked Data, off the main actor, and store that in selectedPhotos (it is the same size the app persists anyway). For the camera, resize the originalImage once before appending.
Expected improvement
Memory held by the add/edit form drops from roughly 50-200 MB per photo to a few MB. That removes the risk of termination during photo selection.
Evidence
if let data  = try? await item.loadTransferable(type: Data.self),
                   let image = UIImage(data: data) {
                    selectedPhotos.append(image)
                }

Photo resize and JPEG encode for up to 10 photos run on the main thread during Save

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:814-833 · reviewer: concurrency-swiftdata
What it is
`save()` is main-actor isolated (default isolation) and loops over `selectedPhotos`, calling `ImageStorage.saveImage` for each one. Each call runs `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` twice (1200px and 200px), runs `jpegData(compressionQuality:)` twice, and writes two files synchronously (ImageStorage.swift 57-71). Camera photos are full-resolution `UIImage`s, and the form allows up to 10.
Why it matters
Saving a bathroom with several camera photos freezes the UI for a noticeable time, because resizing and encoding 12-megapixel images is expensive and nothing here yields.
How to fix
Make `ImageStorage.write`/`resized` `nonisolated` and run the encode and write loop in a detached or `@concurrent` task that returns the file names. Then apply the names to the model back on the main actor.
Expected improvement
Save stays responsive no matter how many photos are attached.
Evidence
for photo in selectedPhotos {
    if let f = ImageStorage.saveImage(photo, bathroomID: bathroom.id) { files.append(f) }
}
Verifier notes (2)

The finding's "814-833" range is close but slightly off, and it cites only the create-mode loop; the edit-mode loop has the same problem. `ImageStorage.write` (lines 57-83) resizes twice (1200px and 200px), JPEG-encodes twice and writes two files synchronously. It also fills the NSCache objects and the static key dictionaries. The 10-photo cap is real (lines 299, 617, 730). Correction on the fix: `write` touches static mutable state (`thumbKeysByBathroom`, `fullKeysByBathroom`), so marking it `nonisolated` as-is would cause a data race. Run only the resize, encode and file write off the main actor (for example in a `@concurrent` helper that returns the data or the file names), and keep the cache and key registration on the main actor.

lowConfirmed map effort: small

PinImages caches one cluster image per distinct count, rendered on the main thread, with no bound and no appearance key

Pitstop/Pitstop/ClusteredMapView.swift:351-385 · reviewer: map-clustering
What it is
The static `PinImages.cache` uses `.cluster(kind, count:)` as its key. With about 6k Candidates, zooming and panning produce many distinct cluster counts (1 to several thousand) across 4 kinds. Each new key runs a SwiftUI `ImageRenderer` synchronously inside `prepareForDisplay` on the main thread, and the result is cached for the life of the process with no eviction. The key also leaves out the trait collection. The first render is reused even after a light/dark appearance change, though system colors such as `.teal`, `.indigo` and `.blue` resolve differently in dark mode.
Why it matters
Zooming out over the seed region renders new images mid-gesture and grows memory without limit for the session. Pins and clusters keep the colors of whichever appearance was active when they were first drawn.
How to fix
Bucket counts for the cluster image (for example exact up to 9, then 10+, 25+, 50+, 100+, 1k+), or draw the count label as a UILabel subview over a cached per-kind background image. A small fixed number of rendered images, no ImageRenderer work during zoom gestures after warm-up, and pins that follow dark mode.

Edit form reads thumbnails from disk synchronously inside the view body

Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift:667-668 · reviewer: images-and-photos
What it is
existingPhotoThumbnail calls the synchronous ImageStorage.loadThumbnail from the view builder. Every time the body is re-evaluated while the cache is cold (first open, or after NSCache evicted entries), it does a file read and decode on the main thread. On a miss, loadThumbnail also calls bathroomDirectory, which runs createDirectory each time. PhotoCarousel and BathroomRow use a .task for this, so the edit form is the odd one out.
Why it matters
Body evaluation should be cheap. The form re-renders on every keystroke and slider change, so a cache miss turns into repeated main-thread disk IO and stutter while editing a Bathroom with many photos.
How to fix
Reuse the ThumbnailCell pattern from PhotoCarousel, a small view with @State image loaded in .task(id: fileName), for the edit form's existing-photo tiles. Separately, have the load and delete paths build the directory URL without calling createDirectory, and keep creation in saveImage only.
Expected improvement
No disk IO during body evaluation, and loads become consistent with the other photo views.
Evidence
let image = editing.flatMap { ImageStorage.loadThumbnail(bathroomID: $0.id, fileName: fileName) }
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift lines 620-690 and Pitstop/Pitstop/ImageStorage.swift lines 1-130, and grepped for loadThumbnail callers. The finding is confirmed. existingPhotoThumbnail (line 667) calls the synchronous ImageStorage.loadThumbnail at line 668, and that call sits in a ViewBuilder ForEach at line 630, so it runs on every body evaluation. On a cache miss, loadThumbnail (ImageStorage.swift:87-95) calls bathroomDirectory, which runs FileManager.createDirectory every time (lines 34-40), then UIImage(contentsOfFile:). loadImage and deleteImage use the same helper. BathroomRow.swift:33 and PhotoCarousel.swift:99 use loadThumbnailAsync inside a .task, so the edit form really is the odd one out. Line numbers are accurate. Scope correction: once a thumbnail is in NSCache, later renders are cheap dictionary lookups. So the repeated IO happens only on a cold cache or after eviction, which the finding does say. Low severity fits. Fix caveat: the project sets SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor (project.pbxproj lines 351 and 389), so ImageStorage and loadThumbnailAsync are main-actor isolated. loadThumbnailAsync just calls the sync loader, so even the .task pattern does the disk read and decode on the main thread. It only moves the work out of body evaluation, to once per cell appearance. To actually get the IO off main, the load would need to be nonisolated or run in a detached task, with the cache access made thread-safe. The thumbKeysByBathroom dictionary is plain mutable static state.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, medium confidence): What I read: AddBathroomView.swift lines 667-668, where existingPhotoThumbnail calls the synchronous ImageStorage.loadThumbnail inside view construction (called from line 630). I also read ImageStorage.swift lines 1-135 (bathroomDirectory at line 34 calls createDirectory on every call, and loadThumbnail, loadImage and deleteImage all go through it), PhotoCarousel.swift around line 99 (.task(id: fileName) calling loadThumbnailAsync), BathroomRow.swift line 33, and project.pbxproj (SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor on the app targets). The core fact holds: this is the only call site that loads a thumbnail synchronously from view-building code. Everywhere else uses .task. The createDirectory-on-read detail is also accurate. The impact is overstated, though: (1) "Repeated main-thread disk IO on every keystroke" is wrong for the normal case. A miss stores the image in the NSCache via setObject, so the next body evaluation is a cache hit. The disk is read again only after NSCache eviction, which is unlikely for a handful of 200px thumbnails, or when the file is missing or undecodable. A failed load is never cached, so a missing file would be retried, with a createDirectory call, on every re-render. That edge case is the only real repeated-IO path. (2) The proposed fix does not move the IO off the main thread. ImageStorage is MainActor-isolated by default because the target sets SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor, and loadThumbnailAsync just calls the synchronous loadThumbnail with no detached task or nonisolated hop. The PhotoCarousel/BathroomRow .task pattern therefore also reads from disk on the main actor. Its only benefits are deferring the read out of body and memoizing it in @State. A real fix would make the load nonisolated, or use Task.detached for the decode, as well as adopting the ThumbnailCell pattern. Separating directory creation from the read and delete paths is a reasonable small cleanup. Overall this is a real but negligible style and consistency issue, not a stutter risk worth "low". I suggest info.
