MiniMapPicker writes the coordinate binding on every camera frame, re-rendering the whole form and scheduling the geocode twice per frame

Pitstop/Pitstop/MiniMapPicker.swift:35-37 · reviewer: location-lifecycle-and-search
What it is
`onMapCameraChange(frequency: .continuous)` assigns `selectedCoordinate` on every animation frame of a pan. That binding is `@State` on AddBathroomView, so every frame re-evaluates the large form body (photo thumbnails included). The parent also has two separate `.onChange` handlers, one for latitude and one for longitude (AddBathroomView.swift lines 174-175). Both fire on the same frame, so `scheduleGeocode` runs twice per frame. Each call cancels and allocates a new Task and runs the `candidateAddress` distance check, even though the 800 ms debounce throws all of that away until the drag stops.
Why it matters
Panning the mini map is the main way to place a pin. Re-rendering a long form with images at 60-120 Hz is wasted work and can make the drag stutter on older devices. The continuous updates buy nothing, because the geocode only runs after the debounce anyway.
How to fix
Switch to `.onMapCameraChange(frequency: .onEnd)`, which writes the binding once when the gesture ends. Or keep the continuous center in local @State inside MiniMapPicker and push it to the binding on `.onEnd`. Then collapse the two parent `onChange` handlers into one, for example by making the coordinate Equatable through a small wrapper.
Expected improvement
One body re-evaluation and one geocode scheduling per drag instead of hundreds, and smoother panning.
Evidence
.onMapCameraChange(frequency: .continuous) { context in
                    selectedCoordinate = context.camera.centerCoordinate
                }
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/MiniMapPicker.swift lines 31-46. Lines 35-37 match the evidence exactly: `.onMapCameraChange(frequency: .continuous) { context in selectedCoordinate = context.camera.centerCoordinate }`. The doc comment on line 5 says the continuous tracking is deliberate, but nothing downstream needs it. I also read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/AddBathroomView.swift: - `selectedCoordinate` is `@State` (line 63) and is read in `body`, so every write re-evaluates the whole form body. - Lines 174-175 have two separate `.onChange` handlers, one for `selectedCoordinate.latitude` and one for `selectedCoordinate.longitude`. Both call `scheduleGeocode(for:)`. During a normal pan both values change, so it runs twice per update. - `scheduleGeocode` (lines 742-755) cancels `geocodeTask`, calls `candidateAddress`, then starts a new Task that sleeps 800 ms. Only the last Task after the drag stops actually geocodes. Corrections to the finding: - `candidateAddress` (lines 783-788) returns early when there is no candidate or no address. The CLLocation distance check only runs when the form was opened from a Candidate. In the plain add flow the per-call cost is just the cancel plus a new Task. - The double call happens only when both latitude and longitude change in the same update. That is typical for a pan, but a purely vertical or horizontal move triggers one handler. On the fix: `.onEnd` works. The address row then updates only after the gesture ends, which is fine because the debounce already waits for that. Collapsing the two `onChange` handlers into one (for example `onChange(of: JumpTarget(lat, lon))`, since that type is already Equatable) is a small, clean change. The impact claims (stutter, 60-120 Hz) are plausible but unmeasured. Low severity is right.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/MiniMapPicker.swift in full, plus AddBathroomView.swift lines 160-320 (the body) and 735-800 (scheduleGeocode, reverseGeocode, candidateAddress). Confirmed: - MiniMapPicker.swift lines 35-37 use `.onMapCameraChange(frequency: .continuous)` and write `selectedCoordinate` on every camera update. A pan normally changes both, so `scheduleGeocode` runs twice per update. Each run cancels geocodeTask, calls candidateAddress (which builds two CLLocations and measures their distance when a Candidate is set), and starts a new Task that sleeps for 800 ms. Only the last of those Tasks survives the drag. Nothing elsewhere mitigates this: no throttling, and no Equatable wrapper. Materiality is minor. SwiftUI only diffs the views, the photo thumbnails are UIImages already in memory, and nobody has measured a stutter. The "can stutter on older devices" claim is speculation, so low severity is right and should not be raised. Fix notes: - `.onEnd` is a valid fix. The 800 ms debounce already makes the address wait until the drag stops, so the user sees no difference. The candidateAddress tolerance for the first-layout re-center still works, because that re-center also produces an end event. - If the coordinate moves to `.onEnd`, the two-handler problem mostly stops mattering (at most two calls per gesture), so collapsing the handlers is optional cleanup. - A simpler way to collapse them than a wrapper type is a single `.onChange(of: MiniMapPicker.JumpTarget(latitude:..., longitude:...))`, or comparing a lat/lon pair, since JumpTarget is already Equatable. - One edge case: with `.onEnd`, tapping Save in the middle of a drag would save the coordinate from the last finished gesture. That is negligible.

Distance formatting copied in four views, CLLocation distance math in several more

Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateRow.swift:79-86 · reviewer: duplication-docs-drift
What it is
BathroomRow, CandidateRow, CandidateDetailView and DetailView each have a private formattedDistance that does the same steps: guard on userLocation, build a CLLocation origin, create an MKDistanceFormatter, set .abbreviated, and format the result. CandidateDetailView adds " away". Straight-line distance is computed four different ways. Bathroom has distance(from:). Candidate has no equivalent, so CandidateRow and CandidateDetailView build CLLocations inline. BathroomFilter.isWithinMaxDistance, ListTabView.sorted (an inline CLLocation per MapSelection) and EmergencyRanker.distance each repeat the math again. Each call also creates a new MKDistanceFormatter, once per row per render.
Why it matters
These are parallel Bathroom and Candidate code paths. A change in one, such as unit style or rounding, won't reach the others, so the list and the detail sheet can show the same place's distance differently. With thousands of Candidate rows, allocating a formatter per row per render also costs time for no benefit.
How to fix
Add one helper, for example `extension CLLocationCoordinate2D { func distance(to other: CLLocationCoordinate2D) -> CLLocationDistance }`, and one shared static MKDistanceFormatter with a `formattedDistance(from:to:) -> String?` function in a small file. Use them in all four views, BathroomFilter.isWithinMaxDistance and ListTabView.sorted. Then delete Bathroom.distance(from:) or make it a one-line wrapper.
Expected improvement
One definition of straight-line distance and its display, about 30 fewer duplicated lines, and one formatter instead of one per row.
Evidence
CandidateRow.swift:79-85 `guard let userLocation else { return nil }\n let origin = CLLocation(latitude: userLocation.latitude, ...)\n let formatter = MKDistanceFormatter()\n formatter.unitStyle = .abbreviated`
The same block appears at BathroomRow.swift:133-139, DetailView.swift:249-255 and CandidateDetailView.swift:150-157.

The "hidden if linked" rule is re-implemented in three places

Pitstop/Pitstop/EmergencyRanker.swift:141-150 · reviewer: duplication-docs-drift
What it is
The rule that a Bathroom's sourceID hides or protects its Candidate is coded three separate times: Candidate.unlinked(from:) at Candidate.swift:73-76 (used by MapTabView and ListTabView), EmergencyRanker.eligible at lines 142-149, and CandidateImporter.apply at lines 49-52 and 76. Each builds its own `Set(...compactMap(\.sourceID))` and filters on it. The ranker also re-implements the dismissed exclusion on its own value type. The ranker's copy is defensible because it is kept pure over plain values, but nothing ties the three copies together.
Why it matters
If the linking rule changes (for example, Bathrooms gain several source IDs, or links become case-normalized), a change made in one place can miss another. Then the map could hide a Candidate that the emergency button still picks, or the importer could delete one the map still shows.
How to fix
Add one small function, `static func linkedSourceIDs(_ sourceIDs: some Sequence<String?>) -> Set<String>`, and call it from all three sites. Keep the per-site filtering. Alternatively, have ContentView pass `candidates.unlinked(from: bathrooms)` into the ranker and keep the ranker's own check only as an asserted invariant.
Expected improvement
One definition of which Candidates are linked, shared by the views, the ranker and the importer.
Evidence
Candidate.swift:74 `let linkedIDs = Set(bathrooms.compactMap(\.sourceID))`
EmergencyRanker.swift:142 `let linkedIDs = Set(visited.compactMap(\.sourceID))`
CandidateImporter.swift:49-51 `let linkedIDs = Set(try context.fetch(FetchDescriptor<Bathroom>(...)).compactMap(\.sourceID))`
Verifier notes (3)

CandidateImporter.swift:49-52 builds the set from a Bathroom fetch with a `sourceID != nil` predicate, and line 76 uses it to protect linked Candidates from deletion. The line numbers and evidence are accurate. Corrections on scope: each copy is a one-line `Set(compactMap(\.sourceID))` over three different input types: SwiftData Bathroom models, the ranker's plain VisitedPlace value type, and an importer fetch running on a background ModelContext. The importer's rule is also a different policy ("protect from deletion", combined with dismissed) from the hiding rule. A shared `linkedSourceIDs` helper would only wrap a single expression, so it gives little protection against drift. The finding admits the ranker's copy is defensible because the ranker is pure. The scenario where the map and the emergency button disagree is real but hypothetical. It would take a schema change to linking, which requires a migration anyway and would naturally touch all sites. LocationManager.locationError is written in four places and never read

Pitstop/Pitstop/LocationManager.swift:13, 62, 70, 77, 82 · reviewer: duplication-docs-drift
What it is
locationError is an observed property set in didFailWithError and in every branch of evaluateAuthorization, with hand-written user-facing strings ("Location access denied. mediumConfirmedverifier suggests: low persistence effort: small

No VersionedSchema or SchemaMigrationPlan, although the shipped schema has already changed shape several times

Pitstop/Pitstop/PitstopApp.swift:13 · reviewer: swiftdata-schema-evolution
What it is
The container is created with ModelContainer(for: Bathroom.self, Candidate.self). No migrationPlan is passed, and grep finds no VersionedSchema or SchemaMigrationPlan anywhere in the app or tests. Git history shows the store has already changed shape implicitly. Commit c8cd26c removed four stored Bathroom properties (verificationCount, lastVerifiedAt, isPrivate, ownerID). Commit 2c62f59 added Bathroom.sourceID and a whole new Candidate entity. Each of those was an unversioned lightweight migration inferred from whatever was on disk.
Why it matters
The next change that is not lightweight (splitting a field, changing a property's type, converting rating values, or making sourceID unique) needs a custom MigrationStage with a frozen copy of the old model. Nothing records today's shape as a named version. If the model drifts again before someone freezes a V1, the frozen copy may not match the store that TestFlight users actually have, and the migration will fail at open, which then hits the fatalError above.
How to fix
Add a SchemaV1: VersionedSchema with nested copies of Bathroom and Candidate that match the current files exactly, plus a PitstopMigrationPlan whose schemas list is [SchemaV1.self] and whose stages list is empty. Pass migrationPlan: PitstopMigrationPlan.self to ModelContainer. Make the app's Bathroom and Candidate typealiases to the latest schema's types so later versions add a new enum alongside the old ones instead of editing these.
Expected improvement
Future schema changes get a defined place to go (a new VersionedSchema plus a lightweight or custom stage) that is anchored to the shape already on users' devices.
Evidence
container = try ModelContainer(for: Bathroom.self, Candidate.self)
Verifier notes (2)

mediumConfirmedverifier suggests: low seed effort: smallfound independently by 3 reviewers

No test guarantees seed sourceIDs are unique, and the importer would reset dismissals if they were not

scripts/seed/build_seed.py:344-364 (importer: Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateImporter.swift 58-74) · reviewer: tests-quality
What it is
`remove_duplicates` drops a record only when a kept record has the same `_group` and lies within 50 m. Two features with the same sourceID (a spider reusing a `ref`) that are more than 50 m apart both survive. The Python tests index records with `by_id`, a dict comprehension that silently collapses duplicates, and `bundledSeedDecodesAndUsesKnownBrands` checks only brand keys. In `CandidateImporter.apply`, the first duplicate record updates the existing Candidate and removes it from `existing`. The second finds nothing and inserts a fresh `Candidate` with `isDismissed: false` under the same `@Attribute(.unique) sourceID`. SwiftData resolves unique-attribute collisions by upserting, so that insert can overwrite the stored row and clear the user's dismissal. The current bundled seed (version 4, 6055 records) has no duplicate IDs. I confirmed that with a quick script, so the risk is latent, not live.
Why it matters
The design requires that the importer never changes the dismissed flag, and the dismissal is user data that exists nowhere else. A future All The Places run with a reused ref would break that rule silently on the next seed refresh. The current tests would not notice.
How to fix
In build_seed.py, check uniqueness before writing, for example `assert len({r['sourceID'] for r in records}) == len(records)` or `sys.exit` with the offending IDs, and add a pytest that feeds a fixture feature with a duplicated ref far from the first. In Swift, add `#expect(Set(seed.records.map(\.sourceID)).count == seed.records.count)` to `bundledSeedDecodesAndUsesKnownBrands`. Optionally make `apply` skip a sourceID it has already seen in this pass.
Expected improvement
A malformed seed fails at build time instead of quietly undoing dismissals on users' devices.
Evidence
if any(
            k["_group"] == record["_group"] and meters_between(k, record) <= DUPLICATE_RADIUS_METERS
            for k in kept
        ):
...
def by_id(seed):
    return {r["sourceID"]: r for r in seed["records"]}

Every claim checks out against the current code. 1. `remove_duplicates` (build_seed.py:344-364) drops a record only when a kept record has the same `_group` and lies within 50 m. Nothing anywhere asserts that sourceIDs are unique: no seen-set in `build_records` (306-341) and no check before writing. 2. `source_id` (line 66-68) is `atp:{spider}:{ref or feature id}`. A collision is therefore only possible within one spider, for example a reused or shared `ref`. That narrows the scope a little. 3. `by_id` in test_build_seed.py:22 is a dict comprehension, so it silently collapses duplicates. 4. `bundledSeedDecodesAndUsesKnownBrands` only checks version, that the seed is non-empty, and that brand keys are known. 5. In `CandidateImporter.apply`, line 59 calls `existing.removeValue(forKey:)` on the first duplicate. The second duplicate then hits the insert branch, which creates a `Candidate` with the default `isDismissed=false` under `@Attribute(.unique) sourceID`. BathroomFilter.applies(to: Bathroom) has no test at all

Pitstop/PitstopTests/BathroomFilterTests.swift:1-160 (impl: Pitstop/Pitstop/BathroomFilter.swift 102-128) · reviewer: tests-quality
What it is
Every test in BathroomFilterTests builds a Candidate through the `candidate(...)` helper. No test constructs a Bathroom or calls `applies(to: Bathroom)`. That overload has 14 independent guards: minimum rating, access/stall/gender set membership, nine boolean must-haves, the bidet mapping `bidetType != .none`, and wait-time membership. None of them is exercised. The spec's filter section says the 24-hour and distance filters apply to both types, but the 24-hour filter is only tested on Candidates.
Why it matters
The Map and List tabs both call this overload on every saved Bathroom (MapTabView.swift:30, ListTabView.swift:48). A wrong comparison here, such as `>` instead of `>=` on rating, a guard bound to the wrong property, or the bidet mapping inverted, would silently hide or show the user's own visits. No test would catch it.
How to fix
Add a parameterized test over a criterion enum, like the existing VisitOnlyCriterion one, that builds two Bathrooms with `Bathroom(name:latitude:longitude:...)`, one matching the criterion and one not, and asserts `applies(to:)` keeps only the matching one. Include a boundary case for `minimumRating` (rating equal to the minimum passes) and a bidet case (`.none` fails, any other type passes). Add one test that `requiresOpen24Hours` filters Bathrooms.
Expected improvement
Regressions in how saved Bathrooms are filtered get caught. Bathroom filtering is the main path for user data in both tabs.
Evidence
func applies(to bathroom: Bathroom) -> Bool {
        if minimumRating > 0 {
            guard bathroom.rating >= minimumRating else { return false }
...
        if requiresBidet                { guard bathroom.bidetType != .none      else { return false } }

Straight-line fallback test computes its inputs from the constant under test

Pitstop/PitstopTests/EmergencyRankerTests.swift:23-27, 144-156 · reviewer: tests-quality
What it is
The `coordinate(minutesAway:)` helper places test points using `EmergencyRanker.fallbackMetersPerMinute`, the same constant the ranker uses to turn distance into minutes. The fallback test (`drivingTimesOverrideStraightLineEstimate`) asserts only ordering, never the minutes it computes. The 20 mph to meters-per-minute conversion (`fallbackMilesPerHour * 1609.344 / 60`) is therefore never checked against an independent value. If the formula were wrong (kilometers instead of miles, or a missing `/ 60`), the helper would scale every test point by the same error and every test would still pass.
Why it matters
The spec lists fallback to straight-line at 20 mph as a required test case. Offline, or when the 3-second deadline expires, this conversion decides every ranking. A unit mistake would make fallback places look much closer or farther than places with real driving times, and no test would fail.
How to fix
Add one test that places a destination at a hand-computed fixed coordinate (about 1609 m due north is about 0.01447 degrees of latitude) with no driving time, and asserts `effectiveMinutes` is about 3.0 (1 mile at 20 mph) plus the known penalty, within a small tolerance. Use the literal 20 mph in the expectation, not the ranker's constant.
Expected improvement
The fallback speed conversion is checked independently, so a unit error fails the tests.
Evidence
let meters = minutes * EmergencyRanker.fallbackMetersPerMinute
        return CLLocationCoordinate2D(latitude: origin.latitude + meters / 111_000, longitude: origin.longitude)
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/PitstopTests/EmergencyRankerTests.swift in full and grepped for the constants in /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/EmergencyRanker.swift. The cited line numbers are correct. What the code does: - The helper at lines 23-27 converts minutes to meters using `EmergencyRanker.fallbackMetersPerMinute`, then divides by 111_000 to get degrees of latitude. - The ranker (EmergencyRanker.swift:105) does the reverse: `distance(...) / fallbackMetersPerMinute`. The constant is defined at lines 38-39 as `fallbackMilesPerHour * 1609.344 / 60`, with 20 miles per hour. - If that constant were wrong by any factor, the helper and the ranker would cancel each other out, and every straight-line test would land on the same minutes. The finding understates how wide this is. The cancellation covers every test that uses the helper without a driving time, including the penalty tradeoff tests at lines 67-105, not just `drivingTimesOverrideStraightLineEstimate` (lines 144-156). Those tests check ordering only. The only tests that assert `effectiveMinutes` (lines 107-120 and 178-187) put the place 0 minutes away and supply a driving time, so they never touch the conversion. No test checks the 20 miles per hour value against an independent number. One detail: a broken conversion would make straight-line places look closer or farther than places with real driving times. It would also skew the balance between travel minutes and the fixed penalties, which matters even when no driving time is known. The suggested fix holds up. Place a destination about 1609.344 m north (about 0.01447 degrees using the 111,000 m per degree figure, or a more exact value, given the ranker measures with CLLocation's own distance). Assert that `effectiveMinutes` is about 3 plus the known penalty, with a tolerance of roughly 0.1 to absorb the latitude approximation.

scripts/seed/test_build_seed.py:77-94 (impl: scripts/seed/build_seed.py 263-294) · reviewer: tests-quality
What it is
The parametrized 24-hour cases cover `24/7`, `Mo-Su`, a two-rule split, a missing day, an `off` override and empty input. Several branches of `expand_days` are never reached. No case covers a wrap-around range such as `Fr-Th 00:00-24:00` (the `% 7` arithmetic), a three-bound selector such as `Mo-We-Fr` (`len(bounds) > 2`), an unknown two-letter token such as `PH off` (not in DAYS, which makes the whole string count as not 24 hours), or a comma list of single days such as `Mo,Tu,We,Th,Fr,Sa,Su 00:00-24:00`.

How to fix
Extend the existing parametrize list with: (`Fr-Th 00:00-24:00`, True), (`Mo,Tu,We,Th,Fr,Sa,Su 00:00-24:00`, True), (`Mo-We-Fr 00:00-24:00`, False), (`24/7; PH off`, False), (`mo-su 00:00-24:00`, False).

        span = (end - start) % 7

Only the all-seven-singles variant is untested, and it adds little. 3. The description says the `off` and other cases leave "several branches of expand_days never reached". To be precise: the unreached paths are the early `return None` inside expand_days (unknown token or more than two bounds) and the wrap-around case of `span`. When `match` is None, is_open_24_hours itself returns False. Each `return False` in is_open_24_hours runs only through the empty-input cases or the `days is None` path, and nothing in the current cases reaches `days is None`. The suggested fix cases are correct for the current code: - `Fr-Th 00:00-24:00` gives True. - `Mo-We-Fr 00:00-24:00` gives False. - `24/7; PH off` gives False (the RULE regex matches "PH off", then expand_days returns None). - `mo-su 00:00-24:00` gives False (the regex accepts lowercase letters, but DAYS does not). This does not duplicate any excluded item. Commit 571273b changed the Swift ranking for unknown hours, not this Python parser.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): I read scripts/seed/build_seed.py lines 250-294 (DAYS, RULE, expand_days, is_open_24_hours) and the parametrized test_is_open_24_hours in scripts/seed/test_build_seed.py (around lines 77-94). VisitOnlyCriterion is hand-maintained, so a new filter criterion missing from requiresVisitDetails is not caught

Pitstop/PitstopTests/BathroomFilterTests.swift:26-49, 128-134 (impl: Pitstop/Pitstop/BathroomFilter.swift 83-97) · reviewer: tests-quality
What it is
BathroomFilter lists visit-only criteria twice: once in `requiresVisitDetails` and once as guards in `applies(to: Bathroom)`. The test enum `VisitOnlyCriterion` is a third copy. If someone adds a new must-have (say `requiresKey`) to the struct and to `applies(to: Bathroom)` but forgets `requiresVisitDetails`, two things go wrong: Candidates still pass `applies(to: Candidate)`, and the filter does not report itself active in `isActive`. The test enum would not include the new case, so the suite stays green.
Why it matters
The spec says rating and amenity filters exclude Candidates whenever they are active. This copy-in-three-places pattern is exactly how that rule silently breaks when a criterion is added.
How to fix
Collapse the duplication in the implementation so one list drives both `requiresVisitDetails` and the Bathroom guards, for example an array of `(isSet: Bool, matches: (Bathroom) -> Bool)` built from the filter's fields. Alternatively, add a test that a Bathroom failing every visit-only criterion still passes `applies(to:)` when `requiresVisitDetails` is false, which catches a guard with no matching flag.
Expected improvement
Adding a filter criterion can no longer accidentally let Candidates through or leave the filter-active badge off.
Evidence
var requiresVisitDetails: Bool {
        minimumRating > 0
        || !accessTypes.isEmpty
...
    nonisolated enum VisitOnlyCriterion: CaseIterable {
        case minimumRating, accessType, stallType, gender, indoor, wheelchairAccessible, changingTable

The parameterized test (128-134) only covers the cases listed in that enum. So the failure the finding describes is real. The unknown-hours rule behind 571273b is decided in untested model and view code

Pitstop/Pitstop/Candidate.swift:55-58 (call site: Pitstop/Pitstop/ContentView.swift 159-167) · reviewer: tests-quality
What it is
`lateNightPenaltySkipsCandidatesWithUnknownHours` passes `isOpen24Hours: nil` straight into the ranker. In the app, nil comes from `Candidate.isOpen24HoursIfKnown`, which infers unknown hours from `openingHours == nil`. The ContentView emergency handler then maps model objects into `VisitedPlace` and `UnvisitedPlace` inline. Neither the inference nor the mapping is tested. Some cases are never exercised end to end, for example a Candidate with `openingHours` set and `isOpen24Hours` false (which should be penalized at night), or a dismissed flag that is never carried across.
Why it matters
The ranker's input contract (nil means unknown hours, and the dismissed flag is passed through) is enforced only by a view function that cannot be unit tested. A change to how the seed encodes missing hours, or to the mapping, would silently change emergency choices at night.
How to fix
Add a Candidate-level test: `openingHours: nil` gives nil, and `openingHours: "Mo-Su 06:00-23:00", isOpen24Hours: false` gives false. Better, move the model-to-ranker mapping out of ContentView into a static helper next to EmergencyRanker (for example `UnvisitedPlace(_ candidate: Candidate)`) and test it with an in-memory Candidate.
Expected improvement
The unknown-hours behavior is covered from model to ranker, not just at the ranker's own boundary.
Evidence
var isOpen24HoursIfKnown: Bool? {
        openingHours == nil ? nil : isOpen24Hours
    }
...
                  reliability: $0.brand.reliability, isOpen24Hours: $0.isOpen24HoursIfKnown,

--fixtures run writes the test seed over the app's bundled CandidateSeed.json and bumps its version

scripts/seed/build_seed.py:11, 30, 391-406 · reviewer: seed-pipeline-python
What it is
`--output` defaults to `Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateSeed.json` whether or not `--fixtures` is passed. The module docstring even documents `python3 scripts/seed/build_seed.py --fixtures DIR` with no `--output`. That invocation builds about 60 records from the small fixture files, sees they differ from the shipped 6,055, and writes them to the app's seed as version 5. Only the pytest helper passes `--output` to a tmp path.
Why it matters
If that file gets committed and shipped to TestFlight, the importer sees version 5 > 4 and deletes every Candidate that is not dismissed and not linked to a Bathroom, about 6,000 pins. Rebuilding from the network afterwards needs another version bump, and the user's devices run through a delete-then-reinsert cycle.
How to fix
When `--fixtures` is given, require an explicit `--output`, or refuse to write to DEFAULT_OUTPUT (parser.error). Fix the docstring example to include `--output`.
Expected improvement
Running the script the way its own docstring shows can no longer replace the shipped seed with fixture data.
Evidence
python3 scripts/seed/build_seed.py --fixtures DIR      # reads DIR/<spider>.geojson ...
parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_OUTPUT)
args.output.write_text(json.dumps(seed, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

No PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy despite using the UserDefaults required-reason API

Pitstop/Pitstop/CandidateImporter.swift:38-45 · reviewer: build-release-config
What it is
The seed importer reads and writes `UserDefaults` to store the last imported seed version. Apple lists UserDefaults as a required-reason API. Apps that use it must declare `NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryUserDefaults` with a reason code (CA92.1, for data the app itself reads and writes) in a PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy privacy manifest. No .xcprivacy file exists anywhere in the repo. The app target generates its Info.plist (GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE = YES), and no privacy manifest resource is added.
Why it matters
App Store Connect flags uploads that use required-reason APIs without a manifest (ITMS-91053) and has said it will reject them. A TestFlight upload, or a later App Store submission, could be blocked by a missing file that takes a few lines to add.
How to fix
Add Pitstop/Pitstop/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy to the app target. Declare NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryUserDefaults with reason CA92.1, set NSPrivacyTracking to false, and leave NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes empty, since nothing leaves the device.
Expected improvement
Uploads pass the required-reason API check, and the manifest records the on-device-only data posture.
Evidence
guard seed.version > defaults.integer(forKey: lastImportedVersionKey) else { return false }
...
defaults.set(seed.version, forKey: lastImportedVersionKey)

lowConfirmedverifier suggests: info release effort: trivial

Build number from local wall-clock minute can repeat or go backwards

scripts/testflight.sh:35 · reviewer: build-release-config
What it is
Each archive sets CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION to `date +%Y%m%d%H%M` in the Mac's local time zone, while MARKETING_VERSION stays 1.0. Two runs in the same minute (for example, a quick rerun after a failed upload, or a `--no-upload` export followed by a real run) get the same build number. When US daylight saving time ends, the 01:00-01:59 hour happens twice, so a build made in the second pass can get a number lower than or equal to the one before it.
Why it matters
App Store Connect rejects a duplicate CFBundleVersion for the same version. That failure comes only after the full archive and export, at upload time.
How to fix
Use UTC so the number always increases (`date -u +%Y%m%d%H%M`). To rule out same-minute collisions too, add seconds or use a monotonic value such as the git commit count combined with the time.
Expected improvement
Build numbers always increase and never collide, so uploads no longer fail on CFBundleVersion.
Evidence
CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION="$(date +%Y%m%d%H%M)" -quiet
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, medium confidence): I read all of /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/testflight.sh. Lines 33-35 pass `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION="$(date +%Y%m%d%H%M)"` to `xcodebuild archive`. That is local time with no `-u`, and the evidence matches line 35 exactly. The header comment on lines 7-8 says "Each build is numbered by the date and time, so every upload is new", so the script relies on this value always being new. Nothing else changes the build number (no agvtool, no git count). I did not check where MARKETING_VERSION is set, so I can't confirm the "stays 1.0" part. The daylight saving case is real. When clocks fall back, local times from 01:00 to 01:59 happen twice, so a build in the second pass can get a number equal to or lower than an earlier upload's. App Store Connect then rejects the upload, and only after the full archive and export. That window is rare but close: US daylight saving time ends 2026-11-01, the day after today's date. Corrections: 1. The same-minute risk is overstated. The timestamp is taken when the archive starts. A rerun after a failed upload starts a new archive only after the earlier archive, export and upload attempt have finished, which normally takes more than a minute, so the two numbers rarely match. The `--no-upload` example is also wrong. That build is never uploaded, so its number cannot clash with anything in App Store Connect. 2. The suggested fix is right: `date -u +%Y%m%d%H%M` removes the daylight saving problem. Adding seconds is optional. Severity stays low.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, medium confidence): I read all of /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/testflight.sh. The file and line are correct: line 35 passes `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION="$(date +%Y%m%d%H%M)"` to `xcodebuild archive`, which uses local time. The header comment (lines 7-8) says the date-and-time number is there so that every upload is new. The same-minute scenarios in the finding mostly don't hold up: - A rerun after a failed upload is harmless. App Store Connect never received the first build, so reusing its number causes no conflict. - A `--no-upload` run followed by a real run is harmless for the same reason: `--no-upload` exits at line 43 before altool runs. - A real same-minute collision needs two successful uploads whose archives both started in the same minute. Runs happen one after another, and a Release archive plus export takes well over a minute, so a second run starts in a later minute. That leaves only two runs going in parallel, which is not realistic for a solo developer. The daylight saving case is real but narrow. When clocks fall back, local 01:00-01:59 happens twice. A build archived in the second pass gets a lower number than one uploaded in the first pass, and App Store Connect rejects a build number that is not higher than the last one for the same marketing version (MARKETING_VERSION stays 1.0). The rejection only shows up at upload time, after the archive and export finish. That needs an upload between 1 and 2am on one night a year. Next occurrence: 2026-11-01, the day after this review. The fix is right and harmless: `date -u +%Y%m%d%H%M` gives a number that always increases, and it is still 12 digits, which CFBundleVersion accepts. Adding seconds isn't needed, given the point above about sequential runs. Severity should be info, not low. The described scope is overstated: the same-minute examples don't cause failures, and the only real trigger is one hour a year.

Upload step looks for the API key somewhere other than ASC_KEY_PATH

scripts/testflight.sh:45-46 · reviewer: build-release-config
What it is
xcodebuild gets the .p8 key file explicitly through `-authenticationKeyPath "$key_path"` from ASC_KEY_PATH. The `altool --upload-app` call passes only the key ID, and as the script's own comment says, altool then looks for the key in ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys (or a few other fixed folders). If ASC_KEY_PATH points anywhere else, archive and export succeed and the upload fails at the very end.
Why it matters
The README says the script reads the key from ASC_KEY_PATH, but the upload step does not. Two copies of the key, or a key in the wrong folder, only show up as a failure after the slowest steps have run.
How to fix
Set `API_PRIVATE_KEYS_DIR="$(dirname "$key_path")"` in the altool command's environment (altool honors it) so both tools use the same key file, or check up front that ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys/AuthKey_$key_id.p8 exists.
Expected improvement
One source of truth for the API key, and no late failures at upload time.
Evidence
# altool finds the key by its ID in ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys.
xcrun altool --upload-app -t ios -f "$ipa" --apiKey "$key_id" --apiIssuer "$issuer"
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/testflight.sh and grepped README.md. The finding is accurate and the line numbers are right. Lines 24-26 read ASC_KEY_PATH, expand ~, and pass it as -authenticationKeyPath to both xcodebuild archive (line 33-35) and -exportArchive (line 38-39). Line 45 is the comment "altool finds the key by its ID in ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys." Line 46 is `xcrun altool --upload-app -t ios -f "$ipa" --apiKey "$key_id" --apiIssuer "$issuer"`, which never passes key_path. The script never checks that the key file exists in altool's search folders, so a key kept only at ASC_KEY_PATH outside those folders gets through archive and export and fails at upload. The header comment (line 2-3) and README line 74 both say the key comes from .env/ASC_KEY_PATH, so the docs do suggest one source of truth that the upload step does not follow. Scope correction: this only breaks setups where ASC_KEY_PATH points outside altool's fixed folders (./private_keys, ~/private_keys, ~/.private_keys, ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys). It is a fragility and doc mismatch, not a current failure, and it is likely fine on the developer's machine today. The fix is reasonable. Setting API_PRIVATE_KEYS_DIR to the key's directory works only if the file is named AuthKey_<ID>.p8, so a preflight existence check is the more robust option to pair with it. Severity low is appropriate.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, medium confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/scripts/testflight.sh in full, plus the README section "Releasing to TestFlight" (line 74). I also checked the ASC_KEY_PATH value in .env (path only, no secret contents). The finding's description is accurate. Line 24 reads ASC_KEY_PATH, and line 26 passes it to both xcodebuild calls through -authenticationKeyPath. The altool call on line 46 passes only --apiKey and --apiIssuer. The comment on line 45 says altool finds the key by its ID in ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys. The cited lines 45-46 are correct. Materiality is overstated, though: - The current .env sets ASC_KEY_PATH to /Users/daniel/.appstoreconnect/private_keys/AuthKey_<id>.p8, and that file exists. Today the two tools use the same file and nothing fails. - The script's comment documents the assumption. - The README sentence is not wrong. The script does read ASC_KEY_PATH and uses it for archive and export. It just doesn't use it for the upload. So this is a latent trap that only matters if the key is ever moved. It is not a live bug, and in this single-developer setup it is close to moot. I'd rate it info rather than low. On the fix: setting API_PRIVATE_KEYS_DIR="$(dirname "$key_path")" on the altool command is reasonable and harmless. Recent altool versions also accept a direct key-file flag (--p8-file-path in Xcode 26-era altool; worth confirming locally with `xcrun altool --help`), which would be the most direct fix. A cheaper option is a preflight check, before archiving, that $key_path equals ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys/AuthKey_$key_id.p8. Any of these makes the failure appear early instead of after the slow steps. None of them conflicts with the intentional designs (secrets stay in .env).

Unused NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription in the generated Info.plist

Pitstop/Pitstop.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:336, 374 · reviewer: build-release-config
What it is

The app picks photos only through SwiftUI `PhotosPicker` (AddBathroomView.swift:615), which runs out of process and needs no photo library permission. A grep finds no PHPhotoLibrary or UIImageWriteToSavedPhotosAlbum use, so the app never asks for library access.

The usage string claims an access the app never requests. That is misleading in the binary's privacy declarations, and App Review sometimes asks about permission strings that have no matching feature.
How to fix
Remove INFOPLIST_KEY_NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription from the Debug and Release app configurations. Keep NSCameraUsageDescription, which UIImagePickerController's camera source needs.

Info.plist declares only the permissions the app actually requests.

Swift 5 mode without SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY leaves the detached seed import unchecked

Pitstop/Pitstop.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:350-354, 388-392 · reviewer: build-release-config
What it is
Both targets use SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0 with SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor and approachable concurrency, but no target sets SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY, so Sendable checking stays at the Swift 5 default (minimal). The one real cross-thread path is `Task.detached` in PitstopApp.importBundledSeed, which runs the `nonisolated` CandidateImporter and creates its own ModelContext. Under minimal checking, the compiler does not check what that closure captures or whether main-actor model objects leak across.
Why it matters
The importer is designed to run off the main thread next to main-actor UI contexts. With minimal checking, a later edit that captures a main-actor value or passes a model object across the boundary compiles silently and becomes a runtime data race in the one store holding the user's only copy of their data.
How to fix
Set SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete on the app and test targets (still Swift 5 mode, so problems show as warnings) and fix what it reports. Or move to SWIFT_VERSION = 6 if the warnings are few.
Expected improvement
The compiler checks the off-main import path, and future concurrency mistakes show up at build time instead of as intermittent crashes.
Evidence
SWIFT_APPROACHABLE_CONCURRENCY = YES;
SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor;
...
SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0;
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, medium confidence): What I read: Pitstop/Pitstop.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj. Lines 350-354 and 388-392 are the app target's Debug and Release configs. Both set SWIFT_APPROACHABLE_CONCURRENCY = YES, SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor and SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0. The test target (lines 410-413 and 432-435) sets SWIFT_APPROACHABLE_CONCURRENCY = YES and SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0 but not SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION. SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY appears nowhere in the project file and no xcconfig is referenced, so the Swift 5 default (minimal) applies. PitstopApp.swift:28-38 does call Task.detached(priority: .utility) and runs the nonisolated enum CandidateImporter (CandidateImporter.swift:24) with the ModelContainer. That matches the finding. Corrections: (1) "Both targets use ... MainActor" is wrong. Only the app target sets default MainActor isolation. The two cited line ranges are the Debug and Release configs of the same app target, not two targets. (2) The scope is overstated. Actor-isolation checking runs in every mode, so touching main-actor state from the detached closure without await is still a compile error. Minimal mode weakens Sendable checking: types that have not declared whether they are Sendable, such as a SwiftData @Model instance or a ModelContext captured into the closure, are not diagnosed. So the real gap is a later edit that passes a non-Sendable model object or context across the boundary. "Captures a main-actor value" is mostly still caught. (3) Today's code only captures the ModelContainer, which is Sendable, and CandidateSeed is explicitly Sendable. There is no current race. This is a hardening note about future edits, so low (or info) severity is right. The suggested fix (SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete on both targets, which still shows problems as warnings in Swift 5 mode) is reasonable. Moving to Swift 6 also works if the warnings are few.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, medium confidence): What I read: Pitstop/Pitstop.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj, PitstopApp.swift (lines 1-39), and CandidateImporter.swift (lines 24-80). I also grepped all Swift sources for detached, nonisolated, Sendable and ModelActor. The settings claim is accurate. App target Debug (350-354) and Release (388-392) have SWIFT_APPROACHABLE_CONCURRENCY = YES, SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor and SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0. The test target's Debug (410-413) and Release (432-435) configs have SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0 and approachable concurrency but no default actor isolation. No config sets SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY, so it stays at minimal. Task.detached in PitstopApp.importBundledSeed (line 29) is the only detached task. There is no current bug. The closure captures only `container` (ModelContainer is Sendable) and uses `.standard` UserDefaults. CandidateImporter is `nonisolated`, CandidateSeed and Record are explicitly Sendable, and importIfNewer creates its own ModelContext. No model object crosses the boundary today. That makes this a defensive hygiene finding, not a defect, so I suggest severity info (low at most). Correction to the description: the compiler does check some of this. Under minimal checking, actor-isolation errors are still enforced in Swift 5 mode, so a synchronous call into a main-actor function or property from the detached closure fails to compile. What minimal mode relaxes is Sendable checking of captured values. The real gap is a later edit that passes a non-Sendable @Model object or main-context ModelContext into the closure. That narrower risk is valid but speculative. The proposed fix is reasonable and low risk: SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete on both targets reports warnings only in Swift 5 mode. Moving to Swift 6 is a larger change.

Persisted enum comment still refers to backend columns that were removed

Pitstop/Pitstop/BathroomAttributes.swift:3-4 · reviewer: swiftdata-schema-evolution
What it is
The header comment says raw values are persisted "locally and in the backend's columns". Commit c8cd26c removed the Supabase backend, and the app is now on-device only. The raw values are only persisted in the SwiftData store, where Bathroom stores BathroomAccess, StallType, GenderType, BidetType and WaitTime as Codable String-backed values. Git history shows the raw strings themselves have never changed, so the warning is still correct for the local store.
Why it matters
The comment is the only guard against renaming or removing a raw value. None of these enums has a tolerant init(from:), so an unknown raw string in the store would fail to decode when the Bathroom is read. Pointing at a backend that no longer exists makes the rule easier to dismiss as obsolete during a cleanup.
How to fix
Reword it to: raw values are persisted verbatim in the SwiftData store. Never rename or remove one; removing a case needs a migration that rewrites existing rows first.
Expected improvement
The comment matches the on-device-only design and states the real constraint on these enums.
Evidence
// Raw values are persisted verbatim, locally and in the backend's columns.
// Don't change them when renaming cases; existing rows would stop decoding.
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): The finding is real. Lines 3-4 of /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/BathroomAttributes.swift say: "// Raw values are persisted verbatim, locally and in the backend's columns." followed by "// Don't change them when renaming cases; existing rows would stop decoding." Commit c8cd26c ("Go local-only: remove Supabase, add TestFlight script") removed the backend, so the words "and in the backend's columns" are out of date. The model file /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/Bathroom.swift (lines 35, 41, 42, 57, 64) stores accessType, stallType, genderType, bidetType and waitTime as these String-backed enums. That makes the warning itself still correct for the SwiftData store. No init(from:) exists in BathroomAttributes.swift, which matches the finding's point that an unknown raw value would fail to decode. Line numbers and quoted evidence are accurate. The fix is a simple rewording, and severity low is right. I did not check the finding's claim that the raw strings have never changed in git history, since the finding does not depend on it.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): The finding is correct. Lines 3-4 of Pitstop/Pitstop/BathroomAttributes.swift say "Raw values are persisted verbatim, locally and in the backend's columns. Don't change them when renaming cases; existing rows would stop decoding." Commit c8cd26c ("Go local-only: remove Supabase, add TestFlight script") is in the history. A grep of Pitstop/Pitstop finds "backend" or "supabase" only in this file, so the comment is the last stale backend reference left in app code. Appendix A — Refuted findings

lowRefuted frontend effort: small

Unknown brand fallback shows the raw seed key and contradicts its own reliability

Pitstop/Pitstop/BrandProfile.swift:69-72 · reviewer: detail-views-ui-state
What it is
`profile(forKey:)` falls back to `displayName: key`, so a brand the Swift table doesn't know appears in the detail header, rows, and VoiceOver pin labels as a snake_case key such as `costco` or `dunkin_donuts`. The fallback also pairs `.uncertain` reliability with `expectedAccess: .free`, so the Reliability section reads "Uncertain: Often needs a code or has no public restroom" right above "Expected: Free". Every brandKey in the current seed is in the table, but the seed and the table are maintained separately in Python and Swift, and nothing checks that they match.
Why it matters
If a brand is added to build_seed.py and the Swift table is forgotten, users see raw identifiers and contradictory guidance, and no crash or test flags it.
How to fix
Make the fallback readable: replace underscores with spaces and capitalize, and use `.keyRequired` to match the uncertain explanation. Better, add a unit test that decodes CandidateSeed.json and asserts every `brandKey` is in `BrandProfile.table`.
Expected improvement
A seed and table mismatch fails a test instead of reaching users, and any fallback still reads as plain text.
Evidence
table[key] ?? BrandProfile(key: key, displayName: key, reliability: .uncertain, expectedAccess: .free)
Verifier notes (2)
  • REFUTE (existence lens, high confidence): I read /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/Pitstop/BrandProfile.swift. Lines 69-72 match the finding: the fallback is `BrandProfile(key: key, displayName: key, reliability: .uncertain, expectedAccess: .free)`. CandidateDetailView.swift:49-54 does render the reliability explanation and then "Expected: ...", so the mismatch would show on screen if the fallback ever ran. The finding's central claim is false, though. It says "nothing checks that they match" and "no crash or test flags it". /Users/daniel/Projects/pitstop/Pitstop/PitstopTests/CandidateImporterTests.swift:175-181 has `bundledSeedDecodesAndUsesKnownBrands`. That test loads the bundled seed and asserts `seed.records.allSatisfy { BrandProfile.table[$0.brandKey] != nil }`. It has been there since commit 2c62f59, and it is exactly the fix the finding proposes. The seed ships inside the app bundle (it is loaded with `loadBundledSeed`), so the seed and the table always ship together. If a brand were added in build_seed.py without a table entry, that test would fail before release. In practice the fallback is a defensive branch that can't be reached. What's left is a cosmetic point: the fallback's display name and the free-access default could be made consistent. That is info-level at most, not a real data-handling defect.
  • REFUTE (impact lens, high confidence): I read Pitstop/Pitstop/BrandProfile.swift. Lines 69-72 match the finding: the fallback is `displayName: key, reliability: .uncertain, expectedAccess: .free`, and CandidateDetailView.swift does show `brand.displayName` and "Expected: \(brand.expectedAccess.displayName)". The finding's main claim is still false. It says the seed and table "are maintained separately in Python and Swift, and nothing checks that they match", but two tests already enforce that they match: - Pitstop/PitstopTests/CandidateImporterTests.swift:174-180, `bundledSeedDecodesAndUsesKnownBrands`, decodes the bundled CandidateSeed.json and asserts `seed.records.allSatisfy { BrandProfile.table[$0.brandKey] != nil }`. This is exactly the "better fix" the finding proposes, and it already exists. - scripts/seed/test_build_seed.py:223-226, `test_every_brand_is_in_the_app_brand_table`, parses BrandProfile.swift and asserts every `build_seed.SOURCES` brand_key is in the Swift table. The seed JSON ships inside the app bundle, so the seed and the table always ship together. With both tests in place, the "forgot the Swift table" scenario is caught before release, and the fallback can't be reached in a shipped build. That leaves a cosmetic inconsistency (uncertain reliability paired with Free access) in dead defensive code, which is info level at most. The other `BrandProfile(... expectedAccess: .free)` constructions in ClusteredMapView.swift:368-373 and CandidateAnnotation.swift:68-69 only build pin and cluster styling for each reliability level, and they never display expectedAccess. I reject the finding because it duplicates existing test coverage and is moot in practice.
lowRefuted backend effort: trivial

@Model classes are main-actor isolated by default but mutated from a nonisolated background importer

Pitstop/Pitstop/Candidate.swift:6-7 · reviewer: concurrency-swiftdata
What it is
The app target builds with `SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor` (project.pbxproj 351, 389). `CandidateImporter` and `CandidateSeed` are explicitly `nonisolated`, but `@Model final class Candidate` and `@Model final class Bathroom` are not. Under the default they are inferred `@MainActor`. `CandidateImporter.apply` runs inside `Task.detached` (PitstopApp.swift 29) and reads `candidate.isDismissed` and `.sourceID`, writes properties through `ReferenceWritableKeyPath`, and calls the `Candidate(...)` initializer, all synchronously from a nonisolated context.
Why it matters
At runtime this is correct, since the background `ModelContext` owns those objects on that thread. But the static isolation says the opposite. In Swift 5 mode these mismatches are only warnings, so real main-actor violations elsewhere drown in the noise, and moving to Swift 6 language mode would turn them into compile errors in the importer.
How to fix
Mark `Candidate` and `Bathroom` (and their nested enums, if needed) `nonisolated` so the models match how SwiftData uses them. Each `ModelContext` already confines them to its own thread.
Expected improvement
Isolation annotations match the real threading model, the importer compiles cleanly under strict checking, and the path to Swift 6 mode opens.
Evidence
@Model
final class Candidate {
...
nonisolated enum CandidateImporter {
...
Task.detached(priority: .utility) {
    let seed = try CandidateImporter.loadBundledSeed()
    try CandidateImporter.importIfNewer(seed, into: container, defaults: .standard)
Verifier notes (2)

Candidate.swift lines 6-7 and Bathroom.swift lines 5-6 are plain `@Model final class` declarations. CandidateImporter and CandidateSeed are `nonisolated`, and PitstopApp.swift line 29 runs the import inside `Task.detached`. The core claim is wrong, though: Candidate and Bathroom do not behave as @MainActor. I checked by compiling the app with xcodebuild into a scratch derived-data folder under /tmp, so nothing in the repo changed. 1) Swift 5 mode, the current setting: BUILD SUCCEEDED with zero Swift warnings. The only warning came from the App Intents metadata tool. So the "warnings that drown out real violations" do not exist. 2) With SWIFT_VERSION=6: the build fails, but nothing fails in CandidateImporter.swift. The `Candidate(...)` init call, the `isDismissed`/`sourceID` reads and the `ReferenceWritableKeyPath` writes all compile cleanly. The "would become compile errors in the importer" prediction is false. 3) The Swift 6 build also shows the reverse problem: `Candidate.swift:65:22: error: call to main actor-isolated static method 'profile(forKey:)' in a synchronous nonisolated context`. That error comes from `var brand: BrandProfile { BrandProfile.profile(forKey: brandKey) }`. It shows the compiler already treats Candidate's members as nonisolated (the @Model macro output overrides the default isolation). What gets inferred MainActor is BrandProfile. So the proposed fix of marking the models `nonisolated` does nothing, and the stated rationale does not hold. If something is worth reporting here, it is a different, latent Swift 6 issue: BrandProfile (BrandProfile.swift) should be `nonisolated`. That code is pure data, and nonisolated model code calls it. The Swift 6 build also hit an unrelated region-based isolation checker error at DrivingTimeLookup.swift:23:27. Neither one is a problem in today's Swift 5 build.
  • REFUTE (impact lens, high confidence): The finding gets the static isolation backwards. What I checked: project.pbxproj does set SWIFT_DEFAULT_ACTOR_ISOLATION = MainActor (lines 351 and 389) with SWIFT_VERSION = 5.0. CandidateImporter.swift has `nonisolated enum CandidateImporter` (line 24), and PitstopApp.swift line 29 runs the import in Task.detached. The main-actor side is BrandProfile. 2. DrivingTimeLookup.swift:23: a region-based isolation checker bug ("Please file a bug"). The importer's reads of isDismissed and sourceID, its ReferenceWritableKeyPath writes, and its Candidate(...) init all compile cleanly in Swift 6 mode. Appendix C — Method & coverage

Scoped reviewers each owned a subsystem × dimension slice, briefed on the project's intentional invariants so designs aren't reported as bugs — only violations of them. A completeness critic audited coverage and spawned extra reviewers (4 gap reviewer(s)). Findings were semantically deduplicated, then adversarially verified: two independent verifiers per finding (an existence lens that re-reads the code, an impact lens that hunts for mitigations and severity inflation), plus a tiebreaker on splits. Every finding cites file:line and a verbatim evidence snippet.
